#### 摘要

Proteus syndrome is caused by an activating AKT1 mutation (c.49G>A, p.Glu17Lys). Many variable features are possible in this mosaic disorder, including: (i) disproportionate, asymmetric, and distorting overgrowth; (ii) bone abnormalities different from those observed in other disorders; (iii) a characteristic cerebriform connective tissue nevus made up of highly collagenized connective tissue; (iv) epidermal nevi in early life, consisting of acanthosis and hyperkeratosis; (v) vascular malformations of the capillary, venous, or lymphatic types; (vi) dysregulated adipose tissue including lipomas, lipohypoplasia, fatty overgrowth, and localized fat deposits; (vii) other unusual features, including bullous lung alterations; specific neoplasms; a facial phenotype associated with intellectual disability and/or seizures, and/or brain malformations; and (viii) deep vein thrombosis, resulting in premature death. Concluding remarks address diagnostic criteria, natural history, management, psychosocial issues, and differential diagnosis.

#### [A] Ultrastructural alterations of human cortical capillary basement membrane in human brain oedema.

- PMID: 24729339
- Domain: Other vascular malformation
- Domain 依据: Other vascular malformation/anomaly expression in article content
- 转化相关性: Strong
- 转化相关性依据: Related vascular malformation with a transferable mechanism or targeted therapy
- 优先级依据: A: related vascular malformation + transferable mechanism/targeted therapy
- 主题: 静脉畸形基础与治疗
- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 期刊: Folia neuropathologica
- 作者: Castejón OJ
- 发表日期: 2014
- DOI: 10.5114/fn.2014.41740
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24729339/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-04

#### 摘要

The capillary basement membranes are examined in severe traumatic brain injuries, vascular malformation, congenital hydrocephalus and brain tumours. They exhibit homogeneous and nodular thickening, vacuolization, rarefaction, reduplication, and deposition of collagen fibers. Their average thickness varied according to the aetiology and severity of brain oedema. In moderate brain oedema the thickness ranged from 71.97 to 191.90 nm in width, and in patients with severe brain oedema it varied from 206.66 to 404.22 nm. The basement membrane complex appears apparently intact in moderate oedema, and shows glio-basal dissociation in severe oedema. In areas of highly increased cerebro-vascular permeability, the basement membrane shows matrix disorganization, reduplication, and bifurcations protruding toward the endothelial cells, and acting as abluminal transcapillary channels. In regions of total brain necrosis, its structural stability is lost showing loosening, dissolution and rupture. Basement membrane swelling is due to overhydration of its protein-complex glycoprotein matrix. The thickening, rarefaction and vacuolization are induced by the increased vacuolar and vesicular transendothelial transport. The degenerated basement membrane areas exhibit a finely granular precipitate interpreted as protein, proteoglycan, glycoprotein, and agrin degraded matrix.

#### [A] Exploratory mRNA and microRNA profiling identifies transcriptomic alterations in the PIK3CA-related overgrowth spectrum.

- PMID: 42992358
- Domain: Other vascular malformation
- Domain 依据: Other vascular malformation/anomaly expression in article content
- 转化相关性: Strong
- 转化相关性依据: Related vascular malformation with a transferable mechanism or targeted therapy
- 优先级依据: A: related vascular malformation + transferable mechanism/targeted therapy
- 主题: 其他血管畸形/血管异常
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: Frontiers in genetics
- 作者: Shiohama T, Uegami M, Kubota Y, Saitsu H, Kato M, Matsushima J, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026
- DOI: 10.3389/fgene.2026.1935186
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42992358/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-08

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: PIK3CA-related overgrowth spectrum (PROS) is a group of disorders caused by somatic mosaic mutations in the PIK3CA gene, characterized by tissue overgrowth affecting multiple organs, including the skin, adipose tissue, blood vessels, bones, and nervous system. Although constitutive hyperactivation of PI3K-AKT-mTOR signaling in PROS promotes abnormal tissue development, remodeling, and segmental overgrowth, the downstream signaling networks are highly complex, and the precise molecular mechanisms underlying tissue overgrowth remain incompletely understood.
METHODS: In this exploratory study, we investigated molecular alterations associated with overgrowth in PROS by profiling mRNA and microRNA expression in fibroblasts derived from affected skin (pv-Fibro) and unaffected skin (non-pv-Fibro) from the same patient. Additionally, fibroblasts from three healthy individuals were pooled and analyzed as a single control sample.
RESULTS: We identified 24,034 human mRNAs using microarray-based gene expression profiling. Ten mRNAs were selected as candidate lesion-associated upregulated genes in pv-Fibro (≥5-fold vs. nc-Fibro and <2-fold change in non-pv-Fibro vs. nc-Fibro). In contrast, three mRNAs were selected as candidate lesion-associated downregulated genes in pv-Fibro (≤0.2-fold vs. nc-Fibro and >0.5-fold in non-pv-Fibro vs. nc-Fibro). For miRNA profiling, 4,603 human microRNAs were analyzed. hsa-miR-146a-5p was markedly upregulated in pv-Fibro (≥5-fold vs. nc-Fibro and <2-fold change in non-pv-Fibro vs. nc-Fibro), while hsa-miR-146b-5p was markedly downregulated (≤0.2-fold vs. nc-Fibro and >0.5-fold in non-pv-Fibro vs. nc-Fibro).
DISCUSSION: The observed changes in mRNA and miRNA expression represent candidate molecular alterations associated with inflammatory and tissue remodeling pathways in PROS pathology.

#### [A] Long-term treatment with sirolimus for pediatric vascular anomalies.

- PMID: 42989251
- Domain: Other vascular malformation
- Domain 依据: Other vascular malformation/anomaly expression in article content
- 转化相关性: Strong
- 转化相关性依据: Related vascular malformation with a transferable mechanism or targeted therapy
- 优先级依据: A: related vascular malformation + transferable mechanism/targeted therapy
- 主题: 其他血管畸形/血管异常
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: Orphanet journal of rare diseases
- 作者: Park J, Kwon H, Kim DY, Kim SC, Namgoong JM, Cho YJ
- 发表日期: 2026 Aug 10
- DOI: 10.1186/s13023-026-04531-y
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42989251/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-08

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Although sirolimus has emerged as a safe and effective treatment modality for unresectable vascular anomalies, comprehensive long-term follow-up data remain scarce in the current literature. This study aims to evaluate the long-term clinical experience of pediatric patients with vascular anomalies who received sirolimus treatment over five years.
METHODS: We retrospectively analysed 17 pediatric patients with vascular anomalies treated with sirolimus over five years in Asan Medical Center. Lesion volumes were measured via 3D volumetric MRI and normalized to body surface area (BSA). Adverse drug effects and therapeutic responses were periodically assessed. Longitudinal response was modeled using generalized additive mixed-effects models (GAMM). Tapering protocols were initiated after 24 months of stability, and alpelisib switch was considered for sirolimus-refractory cases with documented PIK3CA mutations.
RESULTS: With a median follow-up of 78 months, the overall response rate was 81.3%. GAMM analysis demonstrated a significant non-linear volume reduction (p < 0.0001), characterized by a rapid initial response within 12-24 months followed by a sustained plateau. Tapering was successful in 70.6% of patients without disease progression. Three patients with suboptimal responses transitioned to alpelisib; those with sufficient follow-up achieved additional volume reduction within 12 months. Long-term sirolimus use was well-tolerated, with no Grade ≥ 3 adverse events reported.
CONCLUSIONS: Sirolimus provides sustained long-term efficacy and safety in pediatric vascular anomalies. The observed treatment plateau suggests that dose tapering is a viable strategy for sustained responders. Furthermore, genotype-guided transition to alpelisib offers an effective alternative for refractory PIK3CA-mutant cases.

#### [B] ESR Essentials: susceptibility-weighted imaging-practice recommendations by the European Society for Magnetic Resonance in Medicine and Biology.

- PMID: 42965692
- Domain: Other vascular malformation
- Domain 依据: Other vascular malformation/anomaly expression in article content
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Related vascular malformation without a strong targeted mechanism signal
- 优先级依据: B: CCM/related vascular-malformation clinical evidence
- 主题: 静脉畸形基础与治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: European radiology
- 作者: Parillo M, Deistung A, Vaccarino F, Shmueli K, Quattrocchi CC, Rovira À
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 31
- DOI: 10.1007/s00330-026-12988-5
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42965692/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-02

#### 摘要

Susceptibility-weighted imaging (SWI) is a high-resolution 3D gradient-echo (GRE) magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) technique that combines magnitude and phase information to increase sensitivity to blood products, iron, calcification, thrombus, and venous structures. In routine practice, SWI should be interpreted together with source magnitude images, filtered phase images, processed SWI images, and minimum-intensity projection (minIP) images, keeping in mind blooming and other artefacts, as well as vendor-dependent phase conventions. Its most established role is in brain MRI, particularly in: suspected cerebral amyloid angiopathy; baseline and follow-up assessment of amyloid-related imaging abnormalities with haemosiderin deposition (ARIA-H) during anti-amyloid therapy; traumatic brain injury; multiple sclerosis work-up, when assessment of the central vein sign or paramagnetic rim lesions is clinically relevant; neuro-oncology; and vascular malformations. Overall, these constitute the core recommended brain scenarios for SWI supported by moderate to high levels of evidence. Outside neuroradiology (e.g., musculoskeletal, abdominal, breast, pelvic, and cardiac imaging), SWI remains promising but less standardised and should currently be reserved for selected problem-solving indications. KEY POINTS: SWI should be acquired as a high-resolution 3D GRE sequence and interpreted together with source magnitude images, filtered phase images, and post-processed minIP images, keeping in mind blooming and other artefacts, as well as vendor-specific phase conventions. SWI should be included in brain MRI protocols when haemorrhagic or microvascular susceptibility findings are expected to affect diagnosis or management, especially in cerebral amyloid angiopathy, anti-amyloid therapy monitoring, traumatic brain injury, and neuro-oncology or multiple sclerosis work-up. Outside neuroradiology, SWI can be used as a targeted problem-solving adjunct rather than as a routine sequence.

#### [B] Lasers and laser-like devices: part two.

- DOI: 10.1111/ajd.12111
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24433372/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-02

#### 摘要

Part two of this review series evaluates the use of lasers and laser-like devices in dermatology based on published evidence and the collective experience of the senior authors. Dermatologists can laser-treat a wide range of dermatoses, including vascular, pigmentary, textural, benign proliferative and premalignant conditions. Some of these conditions include vascular malformation, haemangioma, facial telangiectases, café-au-lait macules, naevi of Ota, lentigines, acne scarring, rhytides, rhinophyma and miscellaneous skin lesions. Photodynamic therapy with lasers and intense pulsed light is addressed, with particular reference to actinic keratosis and actinic cheilitis. A treatment algorithm for acne scarring based on scar morphology and severity is comprehensively outlined. Following from part one, the various devices are matched to the corresponding dermatological conditions with representative pictorial case vignettes illustrating likely clinical outcomes as well as limitations and potential complications of the various laser and light therapies.

#### [B] Cone-beam CT: vascular applications.

#### 摘要

Cone-beam computed tomography (CBCT) provides three-dimensional rendering of opacified vascular structures that are very useful for catheter guidance and anatomical correlation in many arterial and venous procedures. CBCT has been shown to improve the technical success of transarterial chemoembolization (TACE), which may lead to better outcomes. Similarly, it is useful in planning for selective internal radiation therapy of the liver by defining the treatment volume and preventing misembolization. There is emerging application of CBCT fusion with magnetic resonance angiography (MRA) or computed tomographic angiography (CTA) for use in catheterization or needle puncture of otherwise difficult to opacify vascular structures. CBCT also has a role in needle guidance for type II endoleak embolization, translumbar venous catheter placement, and embolization of vascular malformations.

#### [B] Lymphatic Malformation Involving Cavernous Sinus With Sellar Extension: A Case Report.

- DOI: 10.14791/btrt.2026.0011

- 日报日期: 2026-11-07

#### 摘要

Lymphatic malformations are benign vascular anomalies that most commonly occur in the head and neck region during childhood, and primary intracranial involvement is extremely rare. Lesions involving the cavernous sinus are particularly unusual, given the absence of well-established lymphatic structures in this region. The authors report the case of a 63-year-old man who presented with headache, ptosis, and diplopia. Imaging revealed a homogeneously enhancing mass involving the right cavernous sinus. Partial resection was performed via an endoscopic trans-sphenoidal approach. Histopathological examination demonstrated dilated thin-walled vascular channels lined by endothelial cells with immunoreactivity for podoplanin, suggesting lymphatic endothelial differentiation. This case highlights the diagnostic complexity of cavernous sinus lesions and underscores the importance of careful clinicopathological correlation and consideration of a broad differential diagnosis, including lymphatic and other vascular malformations.

#### [B] Second look at congenital vascular malformations: current classification, diagnostic and treatment principles.

- PMID: 23822946
- Domain: Other vascular malformation
- Domain 依据: Other vascular malformation/anomaly expression in article content

- 发表日期: 2013 Aug
- DOI: 未提供
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23822946/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-08

#### 摘要

Congenital vascular malformations (CVMs) are not rare disorders, with the overall incidence of 1.5%. Due to their complex embryology and various clinical presentations, there was a long lasting confusion among vascular specialists regarding the etiology, classification, basic principles of evaluation and treatment of these anomalies. The introduction of the Hamburg classification and its adoption by experts around the world made further studies of CVMs and comparison of results among different specialists possible. Precise diagnosis of the type and extent of the malformation is paramount for the choice of adequate treatment. In most instances this can be achieved with a detailed history, careful physical examination and a combination of appropriate non-invasive studies. Invasive tests should be reserved for confirmation of diagnosis and serve as a road map for treatment planning. Modern treatment of CVMs is based on a multidisciplinary team approach involving specialists in vascular surgery, interventional radiology, pediatrics, nuclear medicine, orthopedic surgery, plastic and reconstructive surgery and physical therapy. Surgical and endovascular techniques are used in conjunction to minimize morbidity and improve treatment outcomes.

#### [B] Concurrent basilar artery double fenestration with aneurysm and vertebral artery dissection: case report and literature review of rare cerebrovascular abnormalities.

- PMID: 23548267
- Domain: Other vascular malformation

- 日报日期: 2026-11-08

#### 摘要

Many disorders can cause aneurysm and/or dissection of the cerebral arteries, including fibromuscular dysplasia (FMD), connective tissue disorders, cerebral vasculitis, infection, and vascular malformations. Arterial fenestration is a rare congenital finding that can also cause aneurysms, and can rarely dissect and bleed. Treatment of aneurysm and dissection with subarachnoid hemorrhage can be very complicated, and requires case-by-case analysis of the risks and benefits of antithrombotic therapy. To the authors' knowledge, no case of double fenestration of the basilar artery has been reported. This report presents a case of concurring vertebral artery dissection and double fenestration of the basilar artery with aneurysm. The fenestration and FMD are considered possible main contributing causes of this presentation. A literature review of cerebrovascular fenestration and FMD is provided and the relationship between the 2 is considered. Lastly, the use of antithrombotic therapy in the setting of subarachnoid hemorrhage, dissection, and stent placement is discussed.

#### [B] Endoscope-Assisted Transoral Resection for Pharyngeal and Laryngeal Vascular Malformations: A Surgical Technique.

- PMID: 42992577

#### 摘要

Pharyngolaryngeal vascular malformations (PLVMs) are commonly treated with sclerotherapy; however, its efficacy for localized protruding lesions is often limited and may require multiple sessions with a risk of airway edema. We describe an endoscope-assisted transoral technique using flexible-tip endoscopy and tailored laryngoscopic exposure for safe, function-preserving resection. This technique was applied in 7 patients with PLVMs. Surgical exposure was achieved using an FK-WO retractor or curved laryngoscope, and visualization was provided by a flexible-tip rigid endoscope. Resection was performed along the submucosal plane while avoiding intralesional incision. All procedures were completed successfully with minimal blood loss and no postoperative complications. Oral intake was resumed within 3 postoperative days, and no recurrence was observed during follow-up. Endoscope-assisted transoral resection provides a safe and effective treatment for selected PLVMs. This approach allows precise lesion control while preserving function and may represent an alternative to repeated sclerotherapy in appropriately selected patients.

#### 摘要

Ethanol is an effective ablative agent used for the treatment of certain solid organ tumors and vascular malformations (VMs). The egress of ethanol beyond the target tissue can be associated with significant changes to the cardiopulmonary system that can lead to cardiac arrest. This article reviews the contemporary role of ethanol in tumor and VM treatment and discusses the physiological mechanisms of acute pulmonary hypertension and cardiovascular collapse. The importance of periprocedural recognition of the hemodynamic changes that can occur with the use of ethanol and the treatment of this condition are discussed.

#### 摘要

Cutaneous vascular tumors represent a heterogeneous spectrum of lesions with substantial overlapping morphological and immunophenotypic features, posing major challenges to clinical pathological diagnosis. With the widespread adoption of the updated classification proposed by the International Society for the Study of Vascular Anomalies (ISSVA), pathologists have gained an increasingly thorough understanding of vascular tumors, which has greatly facilitated the accurate diagnosis and standardized clinical management of such lesions. Based on the latest revised ISSVA classification of vascular anomalies, this article systematically sorts out the diagnostic difficulties in pathological evaluation of cutaneous vascular tumors, reviews and analyzes these lesions from the aspects of histomorphological features, immunophenotypes and molecular genetics, discusses strategies to further improve the ability of pathologist to recognize and diagnose this category of diseases, and provides pathological evidence for precise clinical diagnosis and treatment.

#### [A] Evaluation and management of neck masses in children.

- PMID: 24695506
- Domain: Other vascular malformation
- Domain 依据: Other vascular malformation/anomaly expression in article content
- 转化相关性: Strong
- 转化相关性依据: Related vascular malformation with a transferable mechanism or targeted therapy
- 优先级依据: A: related vascular malformation + transferable mechanism/targeted therapy
- 主题: 静脉畸形基础与治疗
- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 期刊: American family physician
- 作者: Meier JD, Grimmer JF
- 发表日期: 2014 Mar 01
- DOI: 未提供
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24695506/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-02

#### 摘要

Neck masses in children usually fall into one of three categories: developmental, inflammatory/reactive, or neoplastic. Common congenital developmental masses in the neck include thyroglossal duct cysts, branchial cleft cysts, dermoid cysts, vascular malformations, and hemangiomas. Inflammatory neck masses can be the result of reactive lymphadenopathy, infectious lymphadenitis (viral, staphylococcal, and mycobacterial infections; cat-scratch disease), or Kawasaki disease. Common benign neoplastic lesions include pilomatrixomas, lipomas, fibromas, neurofibromas, and salivary gland tumors. Although rare in children, malignant lesions occurring in the neck include lymphoma, rhabdomyosarcoma, thyroid carcinoma, and metastatic nasopharyngeal carcinoma. Workup for a neck mass may include a complete blood count; purified protein derivative test for tuberculosis; and measurement of titers for Epstein-Barr virus, cat-scratch disease, cytomegalovirus, human immunodeficiency virus, and toxoplasmosis if the history raises suspicion for any of these conditions. Ultrasonography is the preferred imaging study for a developmental or palpable mass. Computed tomography with intravenous contrast media is recommended for evaluating a malignancy or a suspected retropharyngeal or deep neck abscess. Congenital neck masses are excised to prevent potential growth and secondary infection of the lesion. Antibiotic therapy for suspected bacterial lymphadenitis should target Staphylococcus aureus and group A streptococcus. Lack of response to initial antibiotics should prompt consideration of intravenous antibiotic therapy, referral for possible incision and drainage, or further workup. If malignancy is suspected (accompanying type B symptoms; hard, firm, or rubbery consistency; fixed mass; supraclavicular mass; lymph node larger than 2 cm in diameter; persistent enlargement for more than two weeks; no decrease in size after four to six weeks; absence of inflammation; ulceration; failure to respond to antibiotic therapy; or a thyroid mass), the patient should be referred to a head and neck surgeon for urgent evaluation and possible biopsy.

#### [A] Multifaceted neuroprotection of ginsenosides: Mechanisms, nano-delivery advances, and therapeutic potential in central nervous system disorders.

- PMID: 42948208
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Journal of ginseng research
- 作者: Li L, Yu Y, Fan J, Zhang N, Yang J, Li H, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov
- DOI: 10.1016/j.jgr.2026.101149
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42948208/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-02

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Central nervous system (CNS) diseases remain therapeutic challenges due to complex pathology and poor drug penetration across the blood-brain barrier (BBB). Ginsenosides, the active components of Panax ginseng, possess multi-target neuroprotective properties but exhibit low bioavailability.
PURPOSE: This review synthesizes current evidence on ginsenoside mechanisms and evaluates the role of nanotechnology in enhancing their delivery and efficacy for CNS disorders.
STUDY DESIGN: A systematic search was conducted following PRISMA guidelines across PubMed, Web of Science, CNKI, and Google Scholar (2015-present). Included studies investigated ginsenosides in neurological disease models and nano-formulation strategies.
RESULTS: Ginsenosides modulate neuroinflammation, oxidative stress, apoptosis, and calcium homeostasis through pathways involving NF-κB, Nrf2, and Bcl-2/Bax. Nano-delivery systems-such as liposomes, polymeric nanoparticles, and biomimetic vesicles-significantly improve ginsenoside stability, BBB traversal, and brain-targeted accumulation. Functionalization with ligands (e.g., Angiopep-2) or cell membranes further enhances specificity and therapeutic outcomes in ischemic stroke, glioma, Alzheimer's disease, and spinal cord injury models.
CONCLUSION: Nanotechnology-based delivery systems potently augment the neuroprotective potential of ginsenosides, offering a promising strategy for multi-target CNS therapy. Future efforts should focus on translational safety, scalable production, and clinical validation.

#### [A] Persistent Endothelial Glycocalyx Disruption in Sepsis: Translational Evidence for Melatonin-Mediated Vascular Protection.

- PMID: 42927469
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism

- 期刊: Journal of pineal research
- 作者: Martín-Estebané M, Acuña-Fernández C, López-Soto PJ, Hernández-Belsmeisl J, López-Coleto L, Darias-Delbey B, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov
- DOI: 10.1111/jpi.70193
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42927469/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-02

#### 摘要

Sepsis is a life-threatening syndrome driven by a dysregulated host response to infection, with endothelial dysfunction and glycocalyx disruption playing central roles in vascular failure. Despite growing recognition of the glycocalyx as a therapeutic target, its temporal dynamics and potential pharmacological modulation remain poorly understood. We performed a translational study combining longitudinal clinical observations in septic patients with mechanistic investigations in a murine cecal ligation and puncture (CLP) model. Plasma from patients was collected at intensive care unit admission and after clinical recovery to assess inflammatory mediators, coagulation parameters, and glycocalyx-related biomarkers. In parallel, the CLP model evaluated systemic and tissue-specific glycocalyx alterations and the effects of injectable melatonin. Acute sepsis induced marked alterations in multiple glycocalyx-related biomarkers in patients, with several changes persisting despite normalization of organ dysfunction and standard inflammatory indices, indicating ongoing endothelial injury and repair. In the CLP model, sepsis reproduced these alterations, while melatonin treatment significantly attenuated systemic cytokine responses and partially restored circulating and tissue glycocalyx components in an organ- and gene-specific manner. These results highlight persistent, dynamic glycocalyx disruption as a key driver of sepsis pathology and support pharmacological stabilization of the glycocalyx as a promising therapeutic strategy. Melatonin emerges as a potential endothelial-protective agent capable of promoting vascular recovery and homeostasis, providing translational insight for interventions targeting microvascular integrity in sepsis.

#### [A] Multiple sclerosis occurrence with gut microbial metabolites and sex hormones.

- PMID: 42858536
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism

- 作者: ParsaSefat M, Bahar A, Mashhadi MJ, Yosefi S, Khazai M, Tahmasebi H
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov
- DOI: 10.1016/j.msard.2026.107973
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42858536/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-02

#### 摘要

Multiple sclerosis (MS) is a chronic immune and neurodegenerative condition that affects the central nervous system (CNS). Multiple factors, both genetic (including the HLA gene locus) and environmental (Epstein-Barr virus, low vitamin D levels), may contribute to MS development. Growing evidence supports the importance of the gut-brain axis and hormones in disease pathogenesis and progression. This review focuses on identifying the role of specific gut microbial products (short-chain fatty acids, tryptophan derivatives, bile acids, and polyamines) in this process and how they affect the immune system, blood-brain barrier (BBB), and glial cells. The review will also describe key differences between female and male patients with MS, including the higher prevalence in females and the tendency toward a progressive course in males. Estrogens, progesterone, and androgens have immunomodulatory, neuroprotective, and remyelinating activities, and this review will describe their impact on multiple sclerosis based on preclinical and clinical findings from pregnancy, menopause, and hormone replacement therapy. This paper discusses the intersection of these ideas through the "microgenderome" concept, which describes how hormones influence gut microbiota composition and how the microbiota, in turn, modulates hormone metabolism and levels.

#### [A] Differential gene expression in relation to the clinical characteristics of human brain arteriovenous malformations.

- PMID: 24162243
- Domain: Brain AVM / AVM

- 作者: Takagi Y, Aoki T, Takahashi JC, Yoshida K, Ishii A, Arakawa Y, et al.
- 发表日期: 2014
- DOI: 10.2176/nmc.oa2012-0422
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24162243/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-04

#### 摘要

Arteriovenous malformations (AVMs) of the central nervous system are considered as congenital disorders. They are composed of abnormally developed dilated arteries and veins and are characterized microscopically by the absence of a capillary network. We previously reported DNA fragmentation and increased expression of apoptosis-related factors in AVM lesions. In this article, we used microarray analysis to examine differential gene expression in relation to clinical manifestations in 11 AVM samples from Japanese patients. We categorized the genes with altered expression into four groups: death-related, neuron-related, inflammation-related, and other. The death-related differentially expressed genes were MMP9, LIF, SOD2, BCL2A1, MMP12, and HSPA6. The neuron-related genes were NPY, S100A9, NeuroD2, S100Abeta, CAMK2A, SYNPR, CHRM2, and CAMKV. The inflammation-related genes were PTX3, IL8, IL6, CXCL10, GBP1, CHRM3, CXCL1, IL1R2, CCL18, and CCL13. In addition, we compared gene expression in those with or without clinical characteristics including deep drainer, embolization, and high-flow nidus. We identified a small number of genes. Using these microarray data we are able to generate and test new hypotheses to explore AVM pathophysiology. Microarray analysis is a useful technique to study clinical specimens from patients with brain vascular malformations.

#### [A] Overcoming B16F10 Melanoma by Local Co-Delivering Chemotherapeutics and Anti-Angiogenic Agents Using Injectable Thermoresponsive Polypeptide Hydrogels.

- PMID: 42975152
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology

- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 03
- DOI: 10.1021/acsami.6c15683
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42975152/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-04

#### 摘要

The immunosuppressive tumor microenvironment, along with the vascular barrier within B16F10 melanoma, frequently compromises the therapeutic outcomes of antitumor immunotherapy. Hydrogels, possessing three-dimensional network structures, provide a platform for localized drug delivery. Herein, we reported a thermoresponsive polypeptide hydrogel co-loaded with mitoxantrone (MTX) and the angiogenesis inhibitor nintedanib (NE), combined with immune checkpoint blockade therapy, to address these challenges. Results demonstrated that the release of MTX induced immunogenic cell death of tumor cells and released tumor-associated antigens, while the release of NE inhibited angiogenesis, promoted drug permeation and immune cell infiltration, enhanced therapeutic efficacy, and suppressed tumor proliferation and metastasis. In a melanoma-bearing mouse model, the combination of intratumoral injection of MTX/NE-loaded hydrogel with intravenous injection of anti-PD-1 elicited a strong systemic anti-tumor immune response, successfully inhibiting tumor growth and metastasis, while prolonging animal survival. Overall, this study established a hydrogel drug delivery strategy to overcome B16F10 melanoma, demonstrating strong potential in clinical cancer therapy.

#### [A] Cell-Cycle Regulatory Responses in Retinal Degeneration: From Post-Mitotic Homeostasis to Cell-State Transitions and Fate Decisions.

- PMID: 42971418
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Experimental eye research
- 作者: Du W, Ma R, Hong Y, Chen Y, Huang L, Xiao L, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 02
- DOI: 10.1016/j.exer.2026.111309
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42971418/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-04

#### 摘要

Cell-cycle regulatory perturbations are increasingly implicated in retinal degeneration, yet their biological significance varies fundamentally across retinal cell types because proliferative competence differs between terminally differentiated neurons and proliferation-capable retinal populations. While retinal neurons normally maintain permanent cell-cycle exit, Müller glia, microglia, retinal pigment epithelial (RPE) cells, endothelial cells, and pericytes retain varying capacities for quiescence, proliferation, and injury-induced reactivation. Consequently, alterations in cyclins, cyclin-dependent kinases (CDKs), RB-E2F signaling, DNA replication machinery, or checkpoint regulators may represent distinct biological states depending on cellular context. In this review, we examine cell-cycle perturbations across inherited retinal diseases (IRDs), age-related macular degeneration (AMD), diabetic retinopathy (DR), retinal gliosis and inflammation, and retinal organoid models. We critically assess current evidence for neuronal cell-cycle re-entry and propose a practical evidence hierarchy that distinguishes pathway activation, neuronal localization, progression through cell-cycle phases, cell-type-specific causality, and longitudinal cellular fate. We argue that many reported findings reflect incomplete or ambiguous evidence and should not be equated with bona fide neuronal cell-cycle re-entry. We further propose a fate-bifurcation model in which cellular outcomes are determined by the depth and duration of cell-cycle engagement together with cell identity, checkpoint integrity, CDK inhibitor activity, DNA damage burden, metabolic state, and microenvironment cues. Under this framework, limited or checkpoint-restrained engagement may support transient neuronal adaptation or survival, whereas sustained activation may promote senescence-like states, and deeper progression under inadequate checkpoint control may contribute to neuronal dysfunction and death. In proliferation-competent retinal compartments, related pathways may instead drive gliosis, immune expansion, epithelial remodeling, angiogenesis, or senescence. By integrating evidence across retinal diseases and cell types, this state-based framework provides a unified interpretation of apparently conflicting observations and offers a conceptual foundation for developing cell-type- and state-specific therapeutic strategies for retinal degeneration.

#### [A] An Adhesive Aminated Silk Fibroin/Oxidized Alginate Hybrid Hydrogel with Intrinsic Material-Driven Immunomodulatory and Proangiogenic Activities.

- PMID: 42969668
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: ACS applied bio materials
- 作者: Waiyawat J, Watchararot T, Sriwong J, Sangpayap R, Hemstapat R, Tabata Y, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 02
- DOI: 10.1021/acsabm.6c01769
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42969668/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-04

#### 摘要

Effective wound healing requires the coordinated regulation of inflammatory responses and vascular remodeling. However, many bioactive wound dressings rely on exogenous growth factors, therapeutic agents, or cells, which increases formulation complexity and hinders clinical translation. Whether adhesive biomaterials can intrinsically modulate the immune and vascular microenvironments remains poorly understood. Herein, we investigated the intrinsic bioactivity of an adhesive hydrogel composed of aminated silk fibroin and oxidized alginate (ASF/OA), dynamically crosslinked via Schiff-base reactions without any exogenous bioactive additives. Its immunomodulatory and angiogenic potential was evaluated using macrophage assays and the chick chorioallantoic membrane (CAM) model. The ASF/OA hydrogel suppressed tumor necrosis factor-alpha (TNF-α) secretion in a concentration-dependent manner (greater than 90% inhibition at 300 mg mL-1) while upregulating arginase-1 (Arg1) expression at 200-300 mg mL-1, indicating modulation toward a reparative macrophage activation state. Furthermore, the hydrogel promoted macrophage migration, evidenced by pronounced cell elongation and remodeled F-actin cytoskeletal organization. The hydrogel also exhibited no detectable irritation in the hen's egg test (HET)-CAM assay. In the CAM model, ASF/OA significantly increased the number of blood vessels and the vascular index. Histological evaluation revealed host cell infiltration into the peripheral region of the hydrogel, blood vessels beneath and adjacent to the implant, and a continuous hydrogel-CAM interface without obvious fibrous separation. These findings demonstrate that the ASF/OA hydrogel intrinsically modulates macrophage behavior in vitro and enhances neovascularization in the CAM model, effects attributed to its physicochemical and adhesive interfacial properties. This work establishes a simplified, material-driven paradigm for designing multifunctional adhesive biomaterials without relying on external bioactive cues. Further validation using in vivo wound models is warranted to assess the potential of ASF/OA for tissue regeneration.

#### [A] Engineered synbiotic microspheres enable system-level reprogramming of the gut-immune-vascular axis in chronic inflammatory vascular disease.

- PMID: 42981406
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Science advances
- 作者: Shen L, Zhai M, Yang Z, Yu J, Pang Z, Fang Z, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 06
- DOI: 10.1126/sciadv.aeg1718
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42981406/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-05

#### 摘要

Chronic inflammatory vascular diseases are increasingly recognized as systemic disorders sustained by maladaptive cross-organ immunometabolic coupling, yet current therapeutic strategies remain largely lesion-centered and fail to address upstream drivers of disease persistence. Here, we identify the gut-immune-vascular axis as a system-level pathogenic unit that integrates gut microbial dysbiosis, intestinal barrier dysfunction, metabolic imbalance, and vascular immune activation into a self-reinforcing pathological circuit. Through integrated clinical profiling and cross-species validation, we demonstrate that disruption of this axis is characterized by coordinated probiotic depletion, expansion of proinflammatory taxa, translocation of microbial endotoxins, accumulation of microbiota-derived metabolites, and macrophage polarization toward a proinflammatory phenotype along the gut-vascular continuum. To therapeutically target this axis, we engineer enzyme-responsive synbiotic microspheres that codeliver probiotics, a microbial metabolic pathway inhibitor, and a prebiotic within a protective polymeric matrix, enabling site-specific intestinal activation and coordinated microbiota-metabolic modulation. This engineered system restores intestinal barrier integrity, reprograms microbial ecology and metabolite output, suppresses immunometabolic dysfunction, and robustly attenuates pathological vascular remodeling across cellular, murine, and large-animal models, with in-stent restenosis serving as a representative disease context. Collectively, this work establishes axis-oriented immunometabolic reprogramming as a generalizable therapeutic paradigm and highlights engineered synbiotic platforms as scalable tools for system-level intervention in chronic inflammatory diseases.

#### [A] Cross-lineage mesenchymal transition in mesothelioma.

- PMID: 42980853
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Cancer metastasis reviews
- 作者: Wu L, de Perrot M
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 04
- DOI: 10.1007/s10555-026-10394-3
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42980853/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-05

#### 摘要

Mesothelioma is characterized by extreme cellular plasticity, profound stromal remodeling, and near-universal resistance to therapy. While epithelial-mesenchymal transition (EMT) has long been implicated in disease progression, mesothelioma arises within a uniquely heterogeneous cellular ecosystem in which multiple non-epithelial lineages contribute to mesenchymal states. Here, we introduce cross-lineage mesenchymal transition (XMT) as a unifying framework describing the convergence of epithelial, mesothelial, endothelial, immune, hematopoietic, adipocytic, neural, and stem-like cells toward fibroblast-like, extracellular-matrix-producing phenotypes. Mesothelioma is presented as a conceptual framework for studying convergent mesenchymal programming and XMT-related processes, rather than as a definitively proven example of XMT. XMT is driven by shared signaling axes, including TGF-β, Wnt/β-catenin, Notch, hypoxia, and mechanotransduction pathways that erase lineage identity and reinforce mesenchymal programs. Recurrent mesothelioma-associated genomic alterations, particularly loss of BAP1 and CDKN2A, further potentiate XMT by reshaping chromatin accessibility, cell cycle control, metabolic state, and inflammatory signaling, thereby lowering barriers to lineage plasticity. Integrative bulk and single-cell transcriptomic analyses reveal that XMT-associated gene signatures correlate with stromal enrichment, immunosuppressive myeloid and regulatory T cell infiltration, and poor survival in patients with pleural mesothelioma. XMT underlies mesothelioma desmoplasia, immune evasion, and therapy resistance, and represents a tractable vulnerability for stromal, immune, and plasticity-directed therapeutic strategies. Framing mesothelioma through the lens of XMT provides a unifying biological rationale for stromal evolution and identifies actionable pathways-genetic, epigenetic, and microenvironmental mechanisms that may be targeted to overcome plasticity-driven disease progression. This review highlights specific therapeutic strategies, including stromal-targeted approaches aimed at TGF-β signaling and extracellular matrix remodeling, immune-targeted approaches that address myeloid-driven immunosuppression and inflammatory signaling, and plasticity-directed strategies targeting EMT/stemness programs and cellular state transitions.

#### [A] Advances in plant polysaccharide research for neurodegenerative diseases therapy.

- PMID: 42980747
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Molecular biology reports
- 作者: He BH, Yang W, Yang M, Lv X, Zhao JJ
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 04
- DOI: 10.1007/s11033-026-13064-3
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42980747/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-05

#### 摘要

Neurodegenerative diseases (NDs), including Alzheimer's disease (AD), Parkinson's disease (PD), Huntington's disease (HD), amyotrophic lateral sclerosis (ALS), and multiple sclerosis (MS), represent a growing global public health burden with limited therapeutic options. Plant polysaccharides are natural, biocompatible, and multi-target bioactive macromolecules that have emerged as promising candidates for ND intervention. This review systematically summarizes recent progress in elucidating the structural characteristics, structure-activity relationships, and neuroprotective mechanisms of plant polysaccharides. Key structural features such as molecular weight, monosaccharide composition, glycosidic bond configuration, and spatial conformation critically modulate the biological activity of these molecules. Furthermore, we summarize the numerous protective effects of plant polysaccharides against NDs, including inhibiting abnormal protein aggregation, alleviating oxidative stress via the Nrf2/HO-1 pathway, suppressing neuroinflammation through the NF-κB and MAPK signaling pathways, regulating neuronal apoptosis, and modulating the gut-brain axis. Clinical translation challenges, primarily blood-brain barrier penetration, structural heterogeneity, and insufficient clinical validation, are discussed. Future directions include developing advanced delivery systems, conducting large-scale randomized controlled trials, and exploring synergistic combination therapies. Collectively, plant polysaccharides show considerable promise as potential multi-target candidates for the intervention of NDs, although further studies are needed to clarify their therapeutic potential and facilitate clinical translation.

#### [A] From molecular pathways to nanocarrier design: a translational framework for lipid-based nanoparticles in Alzheimer's disease.

- PMID: 42980730
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Discover nano
- 作者: Manna P, Maity A, Roy S, Sahu S, Pradhan S, Ganguly SC, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 04
- DOI: 10.1186/s11671-026-05124-1
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42980730/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-05

#### 摘要

Alzheimer's disease, being a complicated neurodegenerative disoder affects cognitive as well as memory functions. Characterised by amyloid-β aggregation, along with tau hyperphosphorylation, and cholinergic dysfunction, this disease is also associated with oxidative stress and neuroinflammation. Conventional clinical interventions, like acetylcholinesterase inhibitors and novel monoclonal antibodies predominantly rely on symptomatic alleviation of symptoms. Nevertheless, these conventional treatments come across considerable constraints, like temporary effectiveness, lesser therapeutic reach due to the blood brain barrier, apart from additional adverse effects. To overcome these delivery obstacles and achieve intracellular targeting, lipid nanoparticles, such as liposomes, solid lipid nanoparticles, and drugs, that can effectively traverse biological barriers, and facilitate precise intracellular release. This review introduces a translational, pathway-integrated framework that connects key molecular mechanisms of the disease with rational design strategies of lipid nanocarriers. The review also illustrates how essential internal factors, such as lipid composition, ionizable lipid pKa, selection of helper lipids, PEG density, along with methods of endosomal escape, and the protein corona, affect brain targeting efficacy and toxicity. It further focuses on pathway-enforced nano lipidic therapeutic approaches, where nanocarrier design is tailored to cater to specific biological targets. Nano lipidic delivery of siRNA, miRNA, mRNA, monoclonal antibodies, and along with other neuroprotective agents has been emphasised. Additionally, advancements in surface functionalization, intranasal administration, as well as receptor-mediated transcytosis, and focused ultrasound-driven blood-brain barrier penetration have also been highlighted in this review. Therefore, it aims to bridge the gap between molecular pathology and formulation design through alignment of design principles that emphasize on nanocarrier properties and its relevance to disease-specific requirements, an approach that has never been comprehensively addressed in previous literature.Registration number of clinical trial: Not applicable.

#### [A] A pathway-level analysis reveals oxidative and inflammatory vascular stress signatures associated with VEGF inhibition.

- PMID: 42982211
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context

- 作者: da Silva JCL, Whelan G, Martins F, Costa TJ, Neves KB

- DOI: 10.1016/j.vph.2026.107718
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42982211/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-06

#### 摘要

Vascular endothelial growth factor inhibitors (VEGFi) are widely used in the treatment of advanced malignancies, including clear cell renal cell carcinoma (ccRCC), but are frequently associated with cardiovascular toxicities such as hypertension and endothelial dysfunction. The molecular mechanisms underlying these adverse effects remain incompletely understood. In this study, we performed an integrated transcriptomic and functional analysis to characterise vascular stress responses associated with VEGFi. Pre-treatment tumour RNA sequencing data from a public cancer cohort were analysed using Gene Set Enrichment Analysis (GSEA) to identify pathway-level signatures associated with subsequent VEGFi treatment. No individual genes remained significant after multiple testing correction; therefore, emphasis was placed on coordinated biological responses. Functional validation was conducted in human vascular cells treated with a VEGFi to assess reactive oxygen species (ROS) production, cytokine levels and expression, and extracellular vesicles (EVs) release. Pathway analysis revealed significant baseline enrichment of oxidative stress, inflammatory response, and nuclear factor-kappa B (NF-κB) signalling pathways in tumours of patients who subsequently received VEGFi. These baseline associations were complemented by increased vascular cell ROS production and enhanced EVs release in vitro. Inflammatory activation was further confirmed by increased cytokine secretion, despite unchanged messenger RNA expression, indicating regulation at post-transcriptional and secretory levels. Collectively, these baseline tumour signatures and the in vitro vascular findings are consistent with a model in which VEGF inhibition induces a coordinated redox-inflammatory response, which may involve EV-mediated intercellular communication. This mechanism may contribute to vascular dysfunction and cardiovascular toxicity associated with VEGFi-targeted therapies.

#### [A] Dinotefuran aggravates Alzheimer's disease-associated microglial inflammation through the CHUK/NFKB1-mediated NF-κB signaling pathway.

#### 摘要

Neonicotinoid pesticides are widely used systemic insecticides with increasing evidence of neurotoxicity. However, their potential contribution to Alzheimer's disease (AD)-related neuroinflammation remains poorly understood. In this study, seven representative neonicotinoid pesticides were systematically evaluated by integrating physicochemical characterization, target prediction, pathway enrichment analysis, human AD snRNA-seq, molecular docking, molecular dynamics simulations, and experimental validation. Among the seven compounds, dinotefuran was prioritized based on its favorable blood-brain barrier permeability and enrichment in inflammation-related pathways. Integration of target prediction with AD transcriptomic analyses identified CHUK and NFKB1 as key candidate targets associated with NF-κB signaling. snRNA-seq analysis further demonstrated preferential enrichment of NF-κB signaling activity in microglia from AD brains. Molecular docking and molecular dynamics simulations supported potential interactions between dinotefuran and IKKα (CHUK) as well as NF-κB1 (p50). In Aβ-treated BV2 microglia, dinotefuran further enhanced activation of the CHUK/NFKB1-mediated NF-κB signaling pathway, accompanied by increased expression of IKKα, phosphorylation of IκBα and p65, enhanced NF-κB1 activation, and elevated secretion of IL-1β, TNF-α, IL-6, IFN-α, and IFN-β. These pro-inflammatory effects were markedly attenuated by the NF-κB inhibitor BAY11-7082. Collectively, our findings suggest that CHUK/NFKB1-associated NF-κB signaling may contribute to dinotefuran-enhanced inflammatory responses in Aβ-treated BV2 microglia, providing preliminary mechanistic insights into the potential involvement of neonicotinoid pesticides in AD-related neuroinflammation.

#### 摘要

SIGNIFICANCE: Immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs) have transformed cancer therapy by reactivating antitumor immunity and improving outcomes across multiple malignancies. Yet, clinical observations and preclinical studies suggest that ICIs may increase long-term cardiovascular disease risk, including hypertension, atherosclerosis, and thrombosis. The underlying mechanisms remain incompletely defined, highlighting the need for focused investigation into the vascular consequences of immune activation.
RECENT ADVANCES: ICI-induced immune activation disrupts vascular and immune homeostasis through persistent production of reactive oxygen species (ROS), mitochondrial dysfunction, and accumulation of cell-free DNA (cfDNA). Sustained T-cell activation enhances ROS generation, driving DNA damage and senescence in both endothelial and immune cells. ICIs may also promote endothelial-to-mesenchymal transition (EndoMT) through chronic oxidative and inflammatory signaling, contributing to fibrosis, vascular stiffness, and chronic inflammation. Transforming growth factor-beta, although immunosuppressive in many contexts, can drive profibrotic and EndoMT programs within the vasculature. Other cytokines and regulators, including interleukin-17 and regulator of calcineurin 1, have been implicated in vascular remodeling, atherosclerosis, and hypertension.
CRITICAL ISSUES: The relative roles of ROS, mitochondrial dysfunction, cfDNA-driven inflammation, and EndoMT in ICI-associated vascular senescence remain unclear. While inflammation-induced vascular damage is well-recognized, direct ICI-specific mechanisms require stronger evidence. How senescent endothelial and immune cells influence therapy resistance and remodeling of the tumor microenvironment also remains to be defined.
FUTURE DIRECTIONS: Mitochondrial and cytosolic ROS sources-arising from electron leakage during oxidative phosphorylation and NADPH oxidases activity-shape immune signaling and sustain T-cell activation during checkpoint blockade. The DNA damage response kinases ataxia-telangiectasia mutated and ATM- and Rad3-related, activated by oxidative and replicative stress, engage p53-dependent senescence pathways in endothelial and immune cells. Future studies should evaluate biomarkers such as cfDNA and thrombospondin-1 and explore senescence-targeting or redox-modulating interventions that may alleviate cardiovascular toxicity without diminishing antitumor efficacy. This review outlines mechanistic pathways linking ICIs to vascular dysfunction and senescence, integrating preclinical data with emerging clinical insights. It distinguishes established processes from ICI-specific hypotheses and identifies key gaps requiring clinical validation. Antioxid. Redox Signal. 45, 821-839.

#### [A] Synovial Biomarker Modulation and Clinical Response to Umbilical Cord-Derived MSC Treatment in Knee Osteoarthritis: A Case Series Study.

- PMID: 42060495
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 期刊: Cartilage
- 作者: Švajger U, Kolar M, Pompe B, Kobold A, Drobnič M
- 发表日期: 2026 Dec
- DOI: 10.1177/19476035261435793
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42060495/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-07

#### 摘要

ObjectiveUmbilical cord-derived mesenchymal stromal cells (UC-MSCs) are a promising treatment for knee osteoarthritis (KOA). This study aimed to (1) characterize early synovial fluid biomarker changes after intra-articular UC-MSCs therapy in advanced KOA and (2) explore their relationship with short-term clinical outcomes.MethodsIn this prospective, single-arm case series, 15 patients with advanced KOA (Kellgren-Lawrence III-IV) scheduled for total knee arthroplasty received a single intra-articular injection of 20 × 106 allogeneic UC-MSCs. Synovial fluid was aspirated at baseline and approximately 6 weeks post-injection. A panel of 29 soluble biomarkers related to inflammation, matrix remodeling, immune signaling, angiogenesis, and metabolism was quantified using multiplex immunoassays. Clinical status was evaluated with Patient-Reported Outcome Measures (PROMs) before and at a mean of 48 ± 18 days after the injection. Paired differences were analyzed using the Wilcoxon signed-rank tests.ResultsSignificant post-treatment differences were observed in biomarkers associated with extracellular matrix turnover (Matrix Metalloproteinase [MMP]-1, MMP-3, MMP-7, Cartilage Oligomeric Matrix Protein [COMP]), vascular remodeling (Vascular Endothelial Growth Factor [VEGF], Vascular Cell Adhesion Molecule [VCAM]-1), immune modulation (interleukin [IL]-8), and metabolic or structural regulation (Leptin, dickkopf [Dkk]-1). Most PROMs demonstrated significant improvements. These findings describe molecular changes in synovial fluid observed after intra-articular UC-MSC administration.ConclusionsIn this prospective single-arm cohort, intra-articular UC-MSC administration was feasible and well tolerated, and we observed pre-post changes in synovial fluid biomarkers and patient-reported outcomes. These findings do not establish causality and warrant confirmation in controlled studies. The study was registered at Clinicaltrials.gov: Mechanisms of Treatment Effects Using Cultured, Allogeneic Mesenchymal Stromal Stem Cells (MSCs) in Knee Osteoarthritis, NCT06078059, https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06078059?intr=NCT06078059.

#### [A] Neurorehabilitation as an Opportunity for Structured Post-Stroke Lipid Management: A Narrative Review.

- PMID: 42992449
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 期刊: NeuroRehabilitation
- 作者: Fezeu F, Kalle F, Odukudu GD, Mbisike C, Bakinde N
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 06
- DOI: 10.1177/10538135261501053
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42992449/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-07

#### 摘要

Background and PurposeIschemic stroke remains a major cause of death, disability, and recurrent vascular events worldwide. Although lipid-lowering therapy is central to secondary prevention, the transition between acute stroke care and long-term outpatient management remains a vulnerable period during which guideline-directed treatment is frequently delayed, undertitrated, or discontinued. Neurorehabilitation may provide an important opportunity to address this gap. This narrative review summarizes evidence supporting intensive low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (LDL-C) reduction after ischemic stroke and examines how the neurorehabilitation setting may facilitate optimized lipid management.MethodsA literature search was performed in PubMed/MEDLINE, Embase, and the Cochrane Library through March 2026, combining terms across three domains: (1) ischemic stroke and secondary prevention, (2) lipid-lowering therapy, and (3) neurorehabilitation and functional recovery. Included study types encompassed randomized controlled trials, systematic reviews with meta-analyses, clinical practice guidelines, observational cohort and preclinical studies addressing statin pleiotropic effects. Priority was given to landmark randomized trials, major society guidelines, and studies with direct relevance to recurrent stroke prevention, hemorrhagic risk stratification, and rehabilitation-based care delivery.ResultsEvidence from SPARCL, Treat Stroke to Target, FOURIER, and Ez PAVE shows that intensive LDL-C reduction improves secondary stroke prevention. Statins may also affect neuroinflammation, endothelial function, blood-brain barrier integrity, and neuroplasticity, with differences in hemorrhagic stroke risk and safety profiles compared with PCSK9 inhibitors.ConclusionAlthough direct rehabilitation-based randomized trials are lacking, integrating supervised medication titration, adherence monitoring, neuroimaging-guided risk stratification, and patient-centered secondary prevention strategies into neurorehabilitation may represent a practical approach for strengthening secondary stroke prevention and reducing recurrent vascular events.

#### [A] Exploratory mRNA and microRNA profiling identifies transcriptomic alterations in the PIK3CA-related overgrowth spectrum.

- PMID: 42992358
- Domain: Other vascular malformation
- Domain 依据: Other vascular malformation/anomaly expression in article content
- 转化相关性: Strong
- 转化相关性依据: Related vascular malformation with a transferable mechanism or targeted therapy
- 优先级依据: A: related vascular malformation + transferable mechanism/targeted therapy
- 主题: 其他血管畸形/血管异常
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: Frontiers in genetics
- 作者: Shiohama T, Uegami M, Kubota Y, Saitsu H, Kato M, Matsushima J, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026
- DOI: 10.3389/fgene.2026.1935186
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42992358/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-08

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: PIK3CA-related overgrowth spectrum (PROS) is a group of disorders caused by somatic mosaic mutations in the PIK3CA gene, characterized by tissue overgrowth affecting multiple organs, including the skin, adipose tissue, blood vessels, bones, and nervous system. Although constitutive hyperactivation of PI3K-AKT-mTOR signaling in PROS promotes abnormal tissue development, remodeling, and segmental overgrowth, the downstream signaling networks are highly complex, and the precise molecular mechanisms underlying tissue overgrowth remain incompletely understood.
METHODS: In this exploratory study, we investigated molecular alterations associated with overgrowth in PROS by profiling mRNA and microRNA expression in fibroblasts derived from affected skin (pv-Fibro) and unaffected skin (non-pv-Fibro) from the same patient. Additionally, fibroblasts from three healthy individuals were pooled and analyzed as a single control sample.
RESULTS: We identified 24,034 human mRNAs using microarray-based gene expression profiling. Ten mRNAs were selected as candidate lesion-associated upregulated genes in pv-Fibro (≥5-fold vs. nc-Fibro and <2-fold change in non-pv-Fibro vs. nc-Fibro). In contrast, three mRNAs were selected as candidate lesion-associated downregulated genes in pv-Fibro (≤0.2-fold vs. nc-Fibro and >0.5-fold in non-pv-Fibro vs. nc-Fibro). For miRNA profiling, 4,603 human microRNAs were analyzed. hsa-miR-146a-5p was markedly upregulated in pv-Fibro (≥5-fold vs. nc-Fibro and <2-fold change in non-pv-Fibro vs. nc-Fibro), while hsa-miR-146b-5p was markedly downregulated (≤0.2-fold vs. nc-Fibro and >0.5-fold in non-pv-Fibro vs. nc-Fibro).
DISCUSSION: The observed changes in mRNA and miRNA expression represent candidate molecular alterations associated with inflammatory and tissue remodeling pathways in PROS pathology.

#### [A] Immune effector cell-associated neurotoxicity syndrome: what the intensivist needs to know.

- PMID: 42992820
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Minerva anestesiologica
- 作者: Corriero A, Soloperto R, Ferlini L, Massaro F, Salvagno M, Taccone FS
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 06
- DOI: 10.23736/S0375-9393.26.20439-X
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42992820/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-08

#### 摘要

Immune effector cell-associated neurotoxicity syndrome (ICANS) is a significant complication of chimeric antigen receptor T-cell (CAR-T) therapy, a revolutionary treatment for hematologic malignancies. This syndrome, which might affect approximately 30% of CAR-T therapy recipients, presents a wide spectrum of neurological symptoms, ranging from mild confusion to life-threatening cerebral edema. In the intensive care unit (ICU), managing ICANS is critical due to its potential severity. Pathophysiology of ICANS is driven by immune-mediated inflammation disrupting the blood-brain barrier, resulting in cerebral cytokine infiltration and glial cell activation. Its severity is associated with elevated cytokine levels in cerebrospinal fluid and serum, which points to the need for specific biomarkers and predictive tools. Standardized assessments, including the Immune Effector Cell-Associated Encephalopathy (ICE) Score, together with brain imaging and adjunctive diagnostic tests, provide clinicians with essential baseline information for the assessment and monitoring of neurological complications. ICU management focuses on supportive care and pharmacologic strategies. Corticosteroids represent the cornerstone of treatment, effectively targeting neuroinflammation while preserving the antitumor efficacy of CAR-T cell therapy. Anticonvulsants, hyperosmolar agents and advanced neurocritical care measures are also pivotal. Recent advances suggest potential benefits of alternative therapies, such as anti-cytokine agents and innovative immune cell products with reduced neurotoxic risks. Despite advancements, challenges persist, including diagnostic ambiguities, resource allocation, and variability in care protocols. This review highlighted challenges in ICANS management for ICU physicians, intending to underline the importance of early detection, rigorous monitoring, and tailored interventions to improve patient outcomes.

#### [A] Neutrophil membrane-camouflaged ruthenium single-atom nanozymes precisely eliminate neutrophil extracellular traps to reprogram redox-immune microenvironment in intracerebral hemorrhage.

- PMID: 42800424
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: Biomaterials
- 作者: Yu W, Zhang X, Li C, Zeng L, Tian Y, Hong S, et al.
- 发表日期: 2027 Mar
- DOI: 10.1016/j.biomaterials.2026.124649
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42800424/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-08

#### 摘要

Intracerebral hemorrhage (ICH) causes severe secondary brain injury driven by excessive reactive oxygen species (ROS) deposition, neutrophil extracellular traps (NETs) formation, and persistent neuroinflammation. The pathological peptidylarginine deiminase 4 (PAD4)-mediated NETosis and aberrant crosstalk between pro-inflammatory neutrophils and M1-polarized microglia collectively therapeutic strategies remain lacking. Herein, we fabricate a bone marrow-derived neutrophils membrane-camouflaged ruthenium single-atom nanozyme (Ru-SAN) loaded with the PAD4 inhibitor GSK484 (Ru-SAN/G@M) for precise ICH treatment. Taking advantage of neutrophil-derived inflammatory homing properties, Ru-SAN/G@M efficiently crosses the blood brain barrier and specifically accumulates at neuroinflammatory lesions. The Ru single-atom sites exert excellent antioxidant enzyme-mimetic activity to eliminate excess ROS, while encapsulated GSK484 inhibits PAD4 activation and blocks pathological NET formation. This dual modulation disrupts detrimental neutrophil-microglia inflammatory communication, reverses microglial M1 polarization toward the protective M2 phenotype, and alleviates neuronal apoptosis and secondary brain damage. This study develops an integrated catalytic and pharmacological strategy to remodel the redox-immune microenvironment, providing a promising therapeutic paradigm for ICH and other ROS-related neuroinflammatory disorders.

#### [A] Proteus syndrome review: molecular, clinical, and pathologic features.

- PMID: 23992099
- Domain: Other vascular malformation
- Domain 依据: Other vascular malformation/anomaly expression in article content
- 转化相关性: Strong
- 转化相关性依据: Related vascular malformation with a transferable mechanism or targeted therapy

- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 期刊: Clinical genetics
- 作者: Cohen MM
- 发表日期: 2014 Feb
- DOI: 10.1111/cge.12266
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23992099/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-02

#### 摘要

Proteus syndrome is caused by an activating AKT1 mutation (c.49G>A, p.Glu17Lys). Many variable features are possible in this mosaic disorder, including: (i) disproportionate, asymmetric, and distorting overgrowth; (ii) bone abnormalities different from those observed in other disorders; (iii) a characteristic cerebriform connective tissue nevus made up of highly collagenized connective tissue; (iv) epidermal nevi in early life, consisting of acanthosis and hyperkeratosis; (v) vascular malformations of the capillary, venous, or lymphatic types; (vi) dysregulated adipose tissue including lipomas, lipohypoplasia, fatty overgrowth, and localized fat deposits; (vii) other unusual features, including bullous lung alterations; specific neoplasms; a facial phenotype associated with intellectual disability and/or seizures, and/or brain malformations; and (viii) deep vein thrombosis, resulting in premature death. Concluding remarks address diagnostic criteria, natural history, management, psychosocial issues, and differential diagnosis.

#### [A] Klippel-Trenaunay Syndrome Prenatal Diagnosis: Two Case Reports and a Review of the Literature.

- PMID: 42976107
- Domain: Venous malformation
- Domain 依据: Venous or slow-flow malformation expression in article content
- 转化相关性: Strong
- 转化相关性依据: Related vascular malformation with a transferable mechanism or targeted therapy
- 优先级依据: A: related vascular malformation + transferable mechanism/targeted therapy
- 主题: 静脉畸形基础与治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: The American journal of case reports
- 作者: Fang H, Li Y, Zhang J, Xiao J
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 03
- DOI: 10.12659/AJCR.953517
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42976107/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-04

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND Klippel-Trenaunay syndrome (KTS) is an exceedingly rare congenital disorder attributed to a sporadic mutation in the PIK3CA gene, which impairs normal blood and lymphatic vessel development. The syndrome is classically defined by a triad of congenital features: cutaneous port-wine stains, venous malformations, and progressive limb hypertrophy. Prenatal recognition of KTS is uncommon, and its sonographic features can overlap with other congenital vascular anomalies, making early diagnosis challenging. CASE REPORT This report reviews 2 cases of prenatally diagnosed KTS at our institution. In both fetuses, prenatal ultrasound demonstrated marked swelling and thickening of the soft tissues within the right thigh. Within these hypertrophic soft tissues, tortuous and dilated arterial and venous channels were visualized; some veins exhibited a honeycomb-like structural pattern. Color Doppler imaging revealed abundant intralesional blood flow signals. Following induced abortion, gross anatomical examination of both fetuses disclosed reddish skin discoloration over the right lower extremity. The diagnosis of KTS was established based on concordant prenatal ultrasound findings and post-abortion macroscopic anatomical evaluation. CONCLUSIONS The diagnostic cornerstone of prenatal KTS lies in the sonographic detection of disproportionate soft-tissue and bony overgrowth accompanied by characteristic vascular malformations. It is equally imperative to rigorously differentiate these ultrasound findings from those of other overgrowth syndromes and vascular anomalies, and to assess for potential severe complications that may affect perinatal prognosis. An accurate prenatal diagnosis, coupled with multidisciplinary perinatal management involving obstetricians, geneticists, and neonatal specialists, is essential for optimizing pregnancy counseling and improving overall clinical outcomes.

#### [A] A pathway-level analysis reveals oxidative and inflammatory vascular stress signatures associated with VEGF inhibition.

- PMID: 42982211
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Moderate
- 转化相关性依据: Different disease with a highly transferable vascular/endothelial mechanism
- 优先级依据: A: highly transferable cross-domain vascular mechanism
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Vascular pharmacology
- 作者: da Silva JCL, Whelan G, Martins F, Costa TJ, Neves KB
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 04
- DOI: 10.1016/j.vph.2026.107718
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42982211/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-06

#### 摘要

Vascular endothelial growth factor inhibitors (VEGFi) are widely used in the treatment of advanced malignancies, including clear cell renal cell carcinoma (ccRCC), but are frequently associated with cardiovascular toxicities such as hypertension and endothelial dysfunction. The molecular mechanisms underlying these adverse effects remain incompletely understood. In this study, we performed an integrated transcriptomic and functional analysis to characterise vascular stress responses associated with VEGFi. Pre-treatment tumour RNA sequencing data from a public cancer cohort were analysed using Gene Set Enrichment Analysis (GSEA) to identify pathway-level signatures associated with subsequent VEGFi treatment. No individual genes remained significant after multiple testing correction; therefore, emphasis was placed on coordinated biological responses. Functional validation was conducted in human vascular cells treated with a VEGFi to assess reactive oxygen species (ROS) production, cytokine levels and expression, and extracellular vesicles (EVs) release. Pathway analysis revealed significant baseline enrichment of oxidative stress, inflammatory response, and nuclear factor-kappa B (NF-κB) signalling pathways in tumours of patients who subsequently received VEGFi. These baseline associations were complemented by increased vascular cell ROS production and enhanced EVs release in vitro. Inflammatory activation was further confirmed by increased cytokine secretion, despite unchanged messenger RNA expression, indicating regulation at post-transcriptional and secretory levels. Collectively, these baseline tumour signatures and the in vitro vascular findings are consistent with a model in which VEGF inhibition induces a coordinated redox-inflammatory response, which may involve EV-mediated intercellular communication. This mechanism may contribute to vascular dysfunction and cardiovascular toxicity associated with VEGFi-targeted therapies.

#### [A] An endothelial-specific TIE2-L914F mouse model reveals cellular mechanisms of venous malformation progression and recapitulates the patients' response to targeted therapy.

- PMID: 42990562
- Domain: Venous malformation
- Domain 依据: TEK/TIE2 appears with vascular-malformation context
- 转化相关性: Strong
- 转化相关性依据: Related vascular malformation with a transferable mechanism or targeted therapy
- 优先级依据: A: related vascular malformation + transferable mechanism/targeted therapy
- 主题: 静脉畸形基础与治疗
- 来源类型: 近期补位

- 作者: Bischoff LJ, Schrenk S, Barney A, Soroko K, Sherpa C, Simons M, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 30
- DOI: 10.64898/2026.10.29.762550
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42990562/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-07

#### 摘要

TIE2 p.L914F is the most common driver mutation of sporadic venous malformation (VM), a disease characterized by massively enlarged veins. The study of the molecular and cellular mechanisms contributing to TIE2-driven VM has been hindered by the lack of a genetic in vivo model. We generated a mouse system in which TIE2 L914F is expressed postnatally in endothelial cells (EC). Mutant mice develop visible lesions with dilated veins with irregular smooth muscle cell coverage, histologically mimicking patients' VM. With the use of an mCherry reporter we demonstrate that lesions form within the venous vasculature, and mutant ECs undergo clonal expansion fueled by a biphasic mechanism consisting of an early burst of proliferation followed by cell size increase. Targeted treatment with rapamycin partially reduces lesion growth by targeting increased proliferation and prolongs mouse survival, demonstrating this genetic murine model recapitulates the patients' therapeutic response. Together, these findings provide new cellular and mechanistic insights into TIE2 p.L914F-driven venous malformation and establish a genetic platform for preclinical therapeutic studies.
