- 作者: O'Rourke SA, Gonnella G, Kronemberger GS, Petrousek S, Hoey DA, Dunne A, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov
- DOI: 10.1002/jbm.a.70155
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42910446/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-02

#### 摘要

Articular cartilage (AC) repair remains a major clinical challenge due to its limited healing capacity and the inability of current treatment strategies to restore long-term function. Biomaterial-based strategies which support favorable immune responses and appropriate progenitor cell function have shown promise in enhancing cartilage regeneration. We and others have previously demonstrated the capacity of extracellular matrix (ECM)-based materials to direct both innate immune cell behavior and progenitor cell differentiation, with these effects highly dependent on the ECM tissue of origin. Building on these observations, we now aim to further investigate the immunomodulatory properties of AC-derived ECM (AC-ECM) materials. Using primary human macrophages, we demonstrate that AC-ECM material promotes a metabolically active mixed response in macrophages, with elevated expression of reparative growth factors conducive to chondrogenesis. Furthermore, AC-ECM limits inflammatory activation of primary human macrophages, as demonstrated by reduced metabolic reprogramming and inflammatory cytokine production. Functionally, macrophages treated with AC-ECM enhance cartilage regeneration by increasing chondrogenic marker expression in mesenchymal stem cells, while also suppressing angiogenic tube formation in endothelial cells. Overall, this work demonstrates that AC-ECM biomaterials can direct macrophage phenotype toward a pro-chondrogenic state, offering a promising strategy for the development of more effective and durable cartilage repair therapies.

### [C] [Sepsis-induced coagulopathy].

- PMID: 42905264
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Cross-domain vascular mechanism with exploratory CCM relevance
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Die Anaesthesiologie
- 作者: Mirus M, Petrick PL, Schmoch T, Schmitt FCF, Bachler M, Heubner L
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov
- DOI: 10.1007/s00101-026-01748-1
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42905264/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-02

#### 摘要

Sepsis-induced coagulopathy (SIC) represents a frequent and prognostically highly relevant complication of sepsis, characterized by a dysregulated interaction between inflammation, endothelial activation and coagulation. Pathophysiologically, the excessive host immune response to pathogen-associated molecular patterns (PAMP) and damage-associated molecular patterns (DAMP), activation of the complement cascade, formation of neutrophil extracellular traps (NET) and disruption of the endothelial glycocalyx collectively promote a prothrombotic and antifibrinolytic microenvironment, resulting in microvascular thrombosis, organ dysfunction and increased mortality. Approximately 20-25% of critically ill patients with sepsis fulfil the diagnostic criteria for SIC. Current diagnostic approaches rely on scoring systems such as the International Society on Thrombosis and Hemostasis (ISTH)-SIC, ISTH overt-disseminated intravascular coagulation (DIC) and Japanese Society on Thrombosis and Hemostasis (JSTH)-DIC scores; however, their clinical utility remains limited as they predominantly collate hemorrhagic parameters even though sepsis is typically characterized by a thrombotically dominated phenotype. Viscoelastic point-of-care assays enable dynamic bedside assessment of coagulation and fibrinolysis, including the frequently occurring hypofibrinolysis or fibrinolytic shutdown, which is associated with an unfavorable outcome; however, for this specific purpose, these methods have not yet been sufficiently validated or established as a diagnostic standard. Data and recommendations on specific therapeutic options are limited. A consistent pharmacological thromboprophylaxis remains the cornerstone in patients with sepsis, with low-molecular weight heparins demonstrating advantages over unfractionated heparin regarding venous thromboembolic complications. Coagulation modulating treatment, such as antithrombin, recombinant thrombomodulin or activated protein C, have thus far failed to demonstrate consistent survival benefits in large randomized clinical trials. Experimental approaches targeting NET formation, complement activation or fibrinolytic modulation appear mechanistically promising but require further clinical validation. Overall, SIC necessitates an individualized, pathophysiology-driven diagnostic and therapeutic approach integrating inflammatory, endothelial, thrombotic and fibrinolytic alterations.

### [C] Mechanisms underlying exercise-induced pain modulation in people with multiple sclerosis - a scoping review.

- PMID: 42854665
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Multiple sclerosis and related disorders
- 作者: von Glasenapp B, Jungersen RC, Szikszay TM, Wöhler A, Heesen C, Dalgas U
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov
- DOI: 10.1016/j.msard.2026.107967
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42854665/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-02

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND AND STUDY AIM: Multiple Sclerosis (MS) is a prevalent chronic neuroinflammatory disease, characterized by demyelination, inflammation and axonal degeneration. Up to 40% of people with MS (pwMS) experience central neuropathic pain (CNP). Despite numerous debilitating consequences, CNP is often underdiagnosed and undertreated, with limited pharmacological options available. However, exercise is a promising non-pharmacological intervention in the management of pain. This scoping review therefore aims to (a) outline the pathophysiological mechanisms of CNP in MS, (b) summarize the neurophysiological effects of exercise on pain in MS, and (c) identify the mechanistic approaches through which exercise could modulate pain.
METHODS: PubMed, EMBASE, Web of Science, and SPORTDiscus were searched using terms related to MS, exercise, and pain modulation. We included primary quantitative studies as well as relevant secondary evidence, investigating effects or mechanisms of exercise on recurrent, subacute, or chronic pain in pwMS. In the primary studies, pain was assessed through self-reported or objective measures.
RESULTS: Thirty-six articles were included. Mechanistic findings suggest that neuroinflammation, structural and functional central nervous system changes, and alterations in neurotransmitters and hormones contribute to pain in pwMS. Exercise may modulate several of these mechanisms, including inflammatory markers, neurostructural and functional brain changes, and pathways involving endocannabinoid and tryptophan systems.
CONCLUSION: Exercise may exert beneficial effects on central mechanisms relevant to pain modulation in pwMS, including neuroinflammatory pathways, neuroplastic adaptations, and neurotransmitter regulation. However, existing evidence is limited, warranting further high-quality research to fully clarify mechanistic links and establish exercise as a targeted non-pharmacological treatment for CNP in pwMS.

### [C] Inhibition of PDGF signal suppresses the fibrotic scar formation and promotes axonal regeneration in a mouse nigrostriatal lesion model.

- PMID: 42809441
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap

- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: American journal of physiology. Cell physiology
- 作者: Komuta Y, Kimura-Kuroda J, Seko Y, Hongo Y, Suzuki K, Kawano H
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 01
- DOI: 10.1152/ajpcell.00386.2026
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42809441/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-02

#### 摘要

After traumatic injury to the central nervous system (CNS), meningeal fibroblasts migrate into the lesion site and synthesize extracellular matrix (ECM) molecules, leading to the formation of a fibrotic scar (FS). The FS prevents axonal regeneration by releasing inhibitory ECMs, including chondroitin sulfate proteoglycans, slits, and semaphorin 3 A. Platelet-derived growth factor (PDGF) has been implicated in fibrotic diseases, but its specific contribution to FS formation has remained unclear. In this study, we investigated the role of PDGF signaling in scar formation after CNS injury. In the lesioned mouse brain, PDGFRα and PDGFRβ were strongly expressed within the FS, whereas PDGF-B was detected in reactive astrocytes surrounding the lesion. Administration of AG1296, a PDGF signaling inhibitor, into the lesion site significantly reduced FS formation and allowed partial regeneration of transected dopaminergic axons. Complementary in vitro studies using rat meningeal fibroblasts demonstrated that PDGF signaling promoted both proliferation and migration of fibroblasts. Moreover, PDGF stimulation enhanced expression of transforming growth factor-β (TGF-β), a cytokine known to drive ECM deposition and scar formation. These findings provide the first direct evidence that inhibition of PDGF signaling suppresses FS formation and facilitates axonal regeneration after CNS lesioning. Our results suggest that PDGF signaling functions during the early phase of FS development by promoting fibroblast proliferation, migration, and TGF-β expression. Thus, targeting PDGF signaling may represent a promising therapeutic strategy for limiting scar formation and improving neural repair following human CNS injury.NEW & NOTEWORTHY Our research has revealed that PDGF signaling, which is known to be involved in various fibrotic diseases, also contributes to fibrotic scar formation-a barrier to nerve regeneration in the lesioned brain. This signaling plays a role in both the initiation and maintenance of fibrotic scar formation. Indeed, inhibiting PDGF signaling in vivo resulted in the disappearance of the fibrotic scar, thereby enabling nerve regeneration.

### [C] Mechanical immune checkpoints in tumors: Mechanosensing, immunosuppressive networks, and therapeutic modulation.

- PMID: 42805359
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Critical reviews in oncology/hematology
- 作者: Zhao C, Zhang Z, Zhou K, Wang D, Zhu H, Tsai HI
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov
- DOI: 10.1016/j.critrevonc.2026.105629
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42805359/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-02

#### 摘要

Solid tumors undergo significant biomechanical remodeling, which includes ECM stiffening, collagen crosslinking and alignment, increased interstitial fluid pressure, solid stress, vascular compression, and altered viscoelastic properties. These biomechanical abnormalities function not merely as passive structural features but actively influence tumor progression and immune evasion. Emerging evidence introduces the concept of mechanical immune checkpoints, defined as mechanosensory immunoregulatory circuits that link specific biomechanical signals to impaired antitumor immunity through identifiable mechanosensory or mechanotransduction pathways This review first summarizes the mechanisms through which aberrant tumor mechanics contribute to T-cell exclusion, T-cell exhaustion, suppressive myeloid differentiation, and stromal remodeling. Subsequently, it discusses therapeutic strategies, including ECM remodeling, inhibition of mechanotransduction, modulation of mechanosensors and tumor cell stiffness, as well as rational combinations with traditional immune checkpoint blockade. Lastly, the translational maturity of current strategies is critically assessed, highlighting priorities for biomarker-guided combination therapy. By conceptualizing force-sensitive immunosuppressive circuits within a clinically relevant framework, mechanical immune checkpoints may facilitate biomarker-guided patient stratification and rational combination approaches to enhance immunotherapy efficacy in mechanically resistant solid tumors.

### [C] Hepatocyte-derived exosomal miR-122 mediates liver-to-brain crosstalk and neuroinflammation in alcohol-exposed Alzheimer's disease mice.

- PMID: 42975926
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence

- 期刊: Proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences of the United States of America
- 作者: Brezani V, Ortega-Ribera M, Rezagholizadeh N, Joshi R, Brezani V, Babuta M, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 10
- DOI: 10.1073/pnas.2535191123
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42975926/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-04

#### 摘要

Neuroinflammation and microglial activation are common features in Alzheimer's disease (AD) and excessive alcohol consumption. Alcohol induces liver damage and inflammation, and altered serum aminotransferases have been reported in patients with AD. Small extracellular vesicles (sEVs) and their cargo are emerging as mediators of intercellular communication in AD and alcohol-induced liver injury. However, the effects of alcohol use on AD progression remain to be elucidated. In this study, we evaluated the effects of repeated binge drinking on the liver and brain in the APP/PS1 mouse model of AD. Alcohol-exposed APP/PS1 mice showed elevated serum alanine aminotransferase levels, indicating liver injury absent in controls, along with increased pro-inflammatory cytokine expression in both liver and brain. Alcohol increased the biogenesis and release of sEVs in AD mice and in mouse hepatocytes. These sEVs were enriched in mature miR-122-5p that is predominant in hepatocytes. In vivo, miR-122-5p was detected in the brain of alcohol-exposed AD mice suggesting EV-mediated miR-122-5p transfer to the brain. Microglial uptake of hepatocyte-derived sEVs was confirmed in vitro. sEVs from alcohol-treated hepatocytes sensitized BV-2 microglia to LPS stimulation, significantly enhancing CCL2, CXCL2, CCL3, and TNF-α production. Transfection of microglia with a miR-122-5p mimic partially recapitulated the pro-inflammatory sensitization by sEVs from alcohol-exposed hepatocytes, which was reversed by miR-122-5p inhibitor delivery. ADAM10, a miR-122-5p target, was reduced in both wild-type and AD brains after alcohol exposure, with cell-specific effects. These results suggest that alcohol-induced hepatocyte-derived sEVs may modulate liver-to-brain signaling via miR-122-5p and contribute to neuroinflammation in AD.

### [C] Low-dose IL-2 therapy in autoimmune diseases: mechanisms, clinical evidence, and future strategies for precision immune regulation.

- PMID: 42975756
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Clinical reviews in allergy & immunology
- 作者: Fan Z, Zhu W, Yu H
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 03
- DOI: 10.1007/s12016-026-09214-x
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42975756/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-04

#### 摘要

Low-dose interleukin-2 (LD-IL-2) is being developed as an immune-regulatory therapy across rheumatic and autoimmune diseases, but its biology cannot be reduced to regulatory T-cell (Treg) expansion. IL-2 responses are determined by dose and exposure, receptor abundance and binding kinetics, activation state, tissue distribution, and the balance among JAK-STAT5, PI3K-AKT-mTOR, and RAS-MAPK signaling. CD25-high Tregs have a competitive advantage at low concentrations, yet activated conventional T cells, CD8 + T cells, natural killer cells, follicular T-cell subsets, B-cell compartments, and tissue-resident populations may also be modulated directly or indirectly. We therefore synthesize the field through a cross-disease framework linking receptor pharmacology, signaling thresholds, Treg heterogeneity, epigenetic and metabolic fitness, receptor trafficking, and tissue microenvironments to pharmacodynamic, clinical, and durable responses. Disease-specific evidence is critically compared rather than interpreted as a uniform IL-2-deficiency syndrome: systemic lupus erythematosus combines T-cell IL-2 deficiency with Tfh-B-cell and age-associated B-cell pathology; inflammatory arthritis is constrained by synovial cytokine consumption and tissue access; systemic sclerosis by vascular-fibrotic remodeling; inflammatory myopathy by muscle, skin, lung, and microbiome contexts; and neurological autoimmunity by blood-brain-barrier penetration and compartmentalized inflammation. Randomized evidence supports biological activity and preliminary efficacy in selected settings, whereas many indications remain supported by small, uncontrolled studies. Native LD-IL-2 and engineered agonists also require separate evaluation because altered affinity and half-life can change cellular selectivity, immunogenicity, biodistribution, and the risk of chronic receptor overstimulation. We conclude that development should prioritize disease-specific dose-exposure relationships, functional and tissue-resolved biomarkers, rigorous clinical endpoints, and long-term surveillance. This framework positions IL-2 therapy as context-dependent network modulation rather than universal restoration of immune tolerance.

- 作者: Cui H, Han Y, Lu Y, Zheng S, Zhang R, Zhang X, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 03
- DOI: 10.1002/ptr.70474
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42975034/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-04

#### 摘要

Alzheimer's disease (AD) is associated with glutathione (GSH) depletion and redox imbalance in the brain, contributing to hippocampal neuronal damage and synaptic dysfunction and ultimately impairing learning and memory. Salidroside (Sal), the principal active component of Rhodiola rosea L., exhibits antioxidant, anti-inflammatory, and neuroprotective properties; however, its mechanisms of action in AD remain unclear. In this study, an AD-like model was established in C57BL/6 mice using AlCl3/D-galactose. The mice were subsequently treated with normal saline, Sal, or donepezil. A glutamate-induced HT22 cell injury model was established, with ML385 (an inhibitor of Nrf2) used for functional validation. The underlying mechanisms were investigated using behavioral tests, hippocampal histopathological staining, western blotting, immunofluorescence, transmission electron microscopy, Golgi staining, metabolomics, network pharmacology, and molecular docking. Sal significantly improved learning and memory in AD-like mice; reduced hippocampal β-amyloid immunofluorescence, neuronal damage, and brain atrophy; inhibited microglial activation and inflammatory factor release; and attenuated oxidative stress. It also restored synaptic structure and increased the expression of synapse-associated proteins. Metabolomic analysis revealed that Sal ameliorated disturbances in hippocampal GSH metabolism. Sal increased the expression of GSH metabolism-related enzymes, upregulated glutamate-cysteine ligase catalytic subunit and glutamate-cysteine ligase modifier subunit, reduced Keap1 expression, and promoted nuclear Nrf2 accumulation. In summary, Sal attenuated oxidative stress and neuroinflammation and reduced neuronal and synaptic damage in AD-like mouse brains. These effects were associated with alterations in hippocampal GSH metabolism and Keap1/Nrf2/GSH axis activation.

### [C] Spatial mapping of neuroendocrine stress-response programs in head and neck squamous cell carcinoma reveals stromal niches associated with myeloid/Treg enrichment and a reproducible stress-B/plasma coupling.

- PMID: 42972145
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Cross-domain vascular mechanism with exploratory CCM relevance
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Functional & integrative genomics
- 作者: Tang A, Zhong W, Tong Y, Liu Z, Zhu Y, Zhou Z, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 03
- DOI: 10.1007/s10142-026-02101-w
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42972145/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-04

#### 摘要

Chronic stress activates adrenergic and glucocorticoid signaling that shapes anti-tumor immunity in preclinical models, yet spatial evidence in human tumors is lacking. We mapped the spatial activity of neuroendocrine stress-response programs (β2-adrenergic, glucocorticoid receptor, neurotrophin, cAMP/CREB) in spatial transcriptomes of eight treatment-naïve head and neck squamous cell carcinomas (HNSCC; 11,224 Visium spots) and validated compartment associations in TCGA-HNSC (520 tumors). Program activity was spatially autocorrelated in all eight tumors (Moran's I 0.038-0.156), peaked in fibroblast/endothelial stromal niches, and was spatially associated with myeloid/MDSC signatures rather than CD8 T-cell exclusion. In TCGA-HNSC, stress-response scores correlated with regulatory T cell, endothelial, exhaustion and stromal modules (ρ = 0.27-0.33; all BH p < 0.001) but not survival, concentrated in HPV-negative tumors. Whole-transcriptome Open-ST (36,642 cells) confirmed stress-gene measurability and replicated the stress-B/plasma coupling, but the spot-level stromal pattern was not recapitulated within single cells. In a 3D Open-ST volume of a metastatic lymph node (1,054,579 cells), no coupling was conserved (stress-B/plasma partial r + 0.25 to - 0.01, p < 0.001). Purpose-designed 7-plex multiplex immunofluorescence in 20 FFPE oral squamous cell carcinoma (OSCC; oral-cavity HNSCC) resections showed ADRB2-high stroma contained more CD68 + myeloid cells and FoxP3 + regulatory T cells (both p < 0.001), with elevated myeloid PD-L1. In five matched primary-metastatic pairs, stromal ADRB2 and glucocorticoid receptor signaling was attenuated in metastatic lymph nodes (5/5 pairs; median metastatic-to-primary ratios 0.18 and 0.22) while PDGFRα/CCL19 + fibroblastic-reticular architecture was retained, consistent with a tumor-derived stromal mechanism hypothesis. The stress-immunity axis in HNSCC is thus best described as a stromal, myeloid/Treg-enriched niche association, providing a spatial framework for hypothesis-driven testing of adrenergic-pathway interventions.

### [C] Nanobody-Modified Outer Membrane Vesicles Attenuate Alzheimer's Disease-Like Neurodegeneration by Modulating Mitophagy-Associated Phenotypes and cGAS-STING-Related Inflammatory Signaling.

- DOI: 10.1002/adhm.71891
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42971925/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-04

#### 摘要

This study examined anti-transferrin receptor (TfR) nanobody-functionalized bacterial outer membrane vesicles (OMVs) as an engineered platform for improving Alzheimer's disease (AD)-like neuronal injury. Escherichia coli-derived OMVs were characterized for morphology, size distribution, surface charge, protein features, nanobody loading, residual endotoxin levels, endotoxin-associated inflammatory responses, and repeated-exposure immune-safety readouts, and were evaluated in neuronal-related cells and APP/PS1 mice. Engineered OMVs showed receptor-associated uptake and preferential brain-delivery signals, increased HT-22 cell viability, reduced apoptosis, and elevated mitophagy-associated markers including LC3-II, PINK1, and Parkin. OMV treatment also reduced cytosolic mtDNA abundance, DNA/cGAS colocalization, phosphorylation of STING/TBK1/IRF3, and inflammatory-marker expression. PINK1 or Parkin knockdown partially weakened OMV-associated mitochondrial and inflammatory readouts, whereas pharmacological autophagy modulation supported a contribution of autophagy-related processes without establishing selective PINK1/Parkin-dependent mitophagy in vivo. In APP/PS1 mice, OMV administration improved Morris water maze and rotarod performance and alleviated neuroinflammatory and histological injury-related changes. Overall, anti-TfR nanobody-modified OMVs were associated with enhanced receptor-related delivery, mitophagy-linked phenotypes, reduced cytosolic mtDNA/cGAS-STING-related inflammatory signaling, and improved AD-like outcomes, supporting further delivery-system optimization and mechanistic validation.

#### 摘要

This issue of the Biomedical Journal explores therapeutic approaches to neuroinflammation, antimicrobial resistance, and cancer, as well as developments in laboratory testing and prognostic assessment. Experimental findings show that glibenclamide attenuates palmitic acid-induced hippocampal inflammation and memory impairment in mice. In antimicrobial research, CRISPR-Cas9-mediated elimination of resistance genes restores ertapenem susceptibility in resistant Klebsiella pneumoniae isolates, while deep learning offers an approach to interpreting antimicrobial susceptibility tests from images. Oncology articles span molecular characterization, treatment, and prognosis: BK polyomavirus integration and aristolochic acid exposure are associated with distinct molecular features in kidney transplant-associated urothelial carcinomas; bavachinin enhances cisplatin's antitumor activity in oral cancer models; biomarker changes inform prognosis during radium-223 therapy; and external validation supports the MAGIC-D model in advanced biliary tract cancer. Furthermore, research on cartilage homeostasis examines exercise-dependent responses of Prg4-expressing cells. Correspondence addresses combined radiotherapy and systemic treatment for hepatocellular carcinoma and, in a second exchange, stewardship of short-term systemic corticosteroid use.

### [C] Emerging biochemical targets in chronic pain: bridging molecular mechanisms and public health policy in the Indian context.

- PMID: 42969974
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Pain management
- 作者: Sen A, Srinivasan SK, Mohanraj PS, Nagaram S, Kumar R, Velu A, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 02
- DOI: 10.1080/17581869.2026.2754354
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42969974/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-04

#### 摘要

INTRODUCTION: India confronts a chronic-pain paradox widespread under-treatment of pain coexisting with intense restriction of opioid prescribing. India's prevailing opioid-constrained pain-management model, constrained by narcotic legislation, institutional caution, and cultural stigma, is biochemically and clinically inadequate for the maladaptive pathophysiology of chronic pain.
AREAS COVERED: This narrative review synthesizes emerging evidence on non-opioid biochemical targets relevant to chronic pain management in the Indian context, including glial modulators, cytokine inhibitors, NMDA and NK-1 receptor antagonists, selective sodium-channel blockers, TRPV1 antagonists, histone deacetylase inhibitors, and microbiome-based interventions. We discuss the molecular pathophysiology of chronic pain neuroinflammation, central sensitization, and glial activation and integrate preclinical and emerging clinical data. The literature was identified through PubMed, Scopus, and Google Scholar searches up to April 2026, focusing on chronic pain mechanism-based therapeutics relevant to the Indian clinical context.
EXPERT OPINION: Translating mechanism-based pain therapeutics into Indian practice requires concurrent reform across medical education, pediatric pain prevention, and national drug policy. Embedding biochemical literacy in training, expanding equitable access to non-opioid agents, supporting indigenous translational research, and integrating validated Ayurvedic phytochemicals into mechanism-informed care offer a culturally grounded path forward.

### [C] RNA Vaccination Drives Expansion and Brain-Licensing of T Cells to Enhance the Efficacy of Adoptive Cell Therapy in Glioblastoma.

- PMID: 42981468

- 作者: Dobbelstein AD, Chih YC, Acharya B, Aman K, Bakhtiarvand VK, Paul A, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 04
- DOI: 10.1158/0008-5472.CAN-26-0760
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42981468/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-05

#### 摘要

Adoptive cell therapies using chimeric antigen receptor or T cell receptor-transgenic T cells (TCR-T) are effective in treating hematological malignancies but have limited efficacy in solid tumors, particularly brain tumors. A major challenge of adoptive T cell therapy in brain tumors is lack of access to the target antigen, which is necessary for restimulation and persistence. Here, we addressed this challenge by using an mRNA vaccine encoding the cognate antigen to induce in vivo restimulation of transferred TCR-T cells in glioma models. Delivery of mRNA encoding the immunodominant MHC class I ovalbumin epitope SIINFEKL to splenic dendritic cells initiated strong expansion of adoptively transferred ovalbumin-specific OT1 CD8+ T cells to up to 80% of all circulating CD8+ T cells. RNA vaccination markedly improved T cell infiltration and retained interferon-stimulated gene signature in the brain, suppressing glioblastoma growth. The therapeutic efficacy of this approach was further demonstrated in an experimental model for a first-in-class TCR-T cell therapy targeting an HLA-A2-restricted immunodominant epitope of the human glioblastoma stemness antigen PTPRZ1. Co-administration of human dendritic cells transfected with PTPRZ1-mRNA led to rejection of orthotopically implanted primary human gliomas endogenously expressing PTPRZ1 and HLA-A2 after adoptive transfer of PTPRZ1-TCR-T cells. In summary, these findings indicate that mRNA vaccines can boost TCR-T cell therapy against glioblastoma.

### [C] Taurine protects against cadmium-induced neurocardiac injury by modulating inflammatory and apoptotic pathways.

- PMID: 42980712
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence

- 期刊: Journal of molecular histology
- 作者: Coğuplugil A, Kaplan MN, Çiftçi B, Özmen Ö, Kiliç R, Çiftçi O
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 04
- DOI: 10.1007/s10735-026-11029-1
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42980712/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-05

#### 摘要

Cadmium (Cd) is a highly toxic environmental pollutant that induces multi-organ damage, through inflammation and apoptosis, including the brain and heart. Taurine (Tau), a sulfur-containing amino acid, is known to regulate inflammatory signaling and maintain cellular homeostasis. In this study, the protective effects of taurine against cadmium-induced brain and cardiac toxicity were investigated in rats. Adult male Sprague-Dawley rats (3-4 months old, 250-300 g; n = 6/group, 24 rats in total) were divided into four groups: control, cadmium, taurine, and cadmium + taurine. Cadmium chloride (administered intraperitoneally) and taurine (administered orally by gavage) were given for 50 days, at doses of 1 mg/kg and 200 mg/kg/day, respectively. To evaluate cadmium-induced toxicity, serum cytokine levels (TNF-α, IL-6, IL-1β), gene expression of apoptosis- and inflammation-related markers (Bax, Bcl-2, NF-κB), and histopathological alterations were assessed. Cadmium exposure significantly increased serum cytokine levels and upregulated pro-apoptotic and inflammatory gene expression (p < 0.05). These molecular changes were accompanied by marked histopathological alterations in both organs, including, edema, gliosis, and neuronal damage in the brain, as well as myocardial degeneration and structural disruption in cardiac tissue. In contrast, taurine treatment, the taurine treatment significantly reversed the effects of cadmium by reducing serum inflammatory cytokine levels and suppressing both pro-inflammatory and pro-apoptotic gene expression (p <0.05). Similarly, it ameliorated histopathologic damage in both the brain and heart caused by cadmium toxicity. Consequently, taurine treatment significantly protected both the brain and heart tissues by modulating inflammatory and apoptotic pathways induced by cadmium toxicity. These findings indicate that taurine therapy may be used as a potential therapeutic agent in cadmium-induced brain and heart damage.

### [C] Liposomal Delivery of a Riluzole-Butyric Acid Mutual Prodrug Enhances Neuroprotection and Cognitive Recovery in Experimental Alzheimer's Disease.

- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 04

- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42979863/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-05

#### 摘要

Alzheimer's disease (AD) is a progressive neurodegenerative disorder characterized by cognitive decline, impaired synaptic signaling, and persistent neuroinflammation. Current therapies primarily provide symptomatic relief and have limited effects on disease progression. Riluzole, approved for amyotrophic lateral sclerosis, exhibits neuroprotective effects through modulation of glutamatergic signaling, but its poor oral bioavailability limits its therapeutic potential. Butyric acid, a short-chain fatty acid postbiotic produced by gut microbial fermentation, possesses well-established anti-inflammatory and neuroprotective properties. In this study, a mutual prodrug of riluzole and butyric acid was synthesized and encapsulated into liposomes to investigate its effects on drug distribution and preclinical efficacy. The prodrug-loaded liposomes exhibited an average particle size of 322 nm, zeta potential of -7.95 mV, and entrapment efficiency of 43.49%. Oral administration significantly improved cognitive performance and attenuated neuroinflammation and oxidative stress in an AlCl3-induced AD-like rat model. Administration of prodrug-loaded liposomes led to the highest concentrations of riluzole in brain homogenates among the relevant treatment groups. Overall, the prodrug-loaded liposomes demonstrated better therapeutic efficacy than the prodrug suspension, individual drug-loaded liposomes, and their respective conventional formulations. The enhanced therapeutic performance is attributed to the complementary pharmacological actions of riluzole and butyric acid, combined with the ability of the liposomal system to improve drug bioavailability and facilitate cerebral delivery.

- DOI: 10.3791/73040
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42980334/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-05

#### 摘要

Diabetic nephropathy (DN) is a major microvascular complication of diabetes and a leading cause of progressive chronic kidney disease. This retrospective controlled study evaluated whether adding compound alpha-ketoacid tablets to conventional therapy improves renal function, inflammatory response, fibrosis-related biomarkers, oxidative stress status, therapeutic efficacy, and safety in patients with diabetic nephropathy. A total of 312 patients treated from October 2022 to June 2023 were included. The control group received conventional treatment, including glycemic control, antihypertensive therapy, lipid regulation, low-protein dietary guidance, and basic nutritional support. The experimental group received compound alpha-ketoacid tablets in addition to conventional therapy for 3 months. Compared with conventional therapy alone, combined therapy produced a higher total effective rate and greater improvements in blood urea nitrogen (BUN), serum creatinine (Cr), estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR), homocysteine (Hcy), hepatocyte growth factor (HGF), vascular endothelial growth factor (VEGF), C-reactive protein (CRP), procalcitonin (PCT), interleukin-6 (IL-6), total antioxidant capacity (T-AOC), superoxide dismutase (SOD), and malondialdehyde (MDA). The incidence of adverse drug reactions did not differ significantly between groups. These findings suggest that supplementation with alpha-ketoacid compounds may be a useful adjunctive strategy to improve renal and inflammatory outcomes in selected patients with diabetic nephropathy without increasing safety risks.

- DOI: 10.1038/s41591-026-04743-6
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42977756/

#### 摘要

Amsokigene autoleucel (amsoki-cel, OBX-115) is a tumor-infiltrating lymphocyte (TIL) cell therapy engineered to express acetazolamide (ACZ)-regulatable membrane-bound interleukin-15, abrogating the need for interleukin-2. In two single-arm trials (completed phase 1 single-center first-in-human (FIH) and ongoing phase 1/2 multicenter Agni-01), patients with immune checkpoint inhibitor (ICI)-resistant advanced melanoma received lymphodepletion and varying doses and schedules of amsoki-cel and ACZ. The primary objective of FIH and Agni-01 phase 1 was safety, tolerability and recommended phase 2 dose (RP2D); the primary objective of Agni-01 phase 2 was objective response rate (ORR) at the RP2D. The first patient treated experienced rapid lymphocyte expansion and vasogenic edema of a previously irradiated brain metastasis, prompting protocol modifications and dose deescalation in FIH, including 80% reduction in maximum amsoki-cel dose and 75% reduction in ACZ dose. After preliminary demonstration of safety at the lower dose, doses were increased in FIH, and reescalation was initiated in Agni-01. Across studies, 37 patients were treated (FIH, n = 17; Agni-01, n = 20). Post hoc pooled analysis demonstrated no dose-limiting toxicities, ICU care or treatment-related mortality. Most common grade 3 or higher treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) included neutropenia (64.9% (24/37)), leukopenia/lymphopenia (56.8% (21/37) each) and anemia/thrombocytopenia (37.8% (14/37) each). Serious TEAEs were reported for 10 patients, including eight patients with grade 3 or higher events (non-hematologic, 13.5% (5/37); hematologic, 13.5% (5/37)). RP2D was selected based on overall safety and tolerability as well as preliminary anti-tumor activity in Agni-01 phase 1. The phase 2 preliminary ORR was 66.7% (6/9). In total, 15 patients were treated at RP2D and comprise the Key Efficacy Set (Agni-01: phase 1, n = 6; phase 2, n = 9), of which 10 patients (66.7%; 95% confidence interval 38-88%) achieved objective responses (including two complete responses). Amsoki-cel demonstrated favorable safety and promising early clinical activity in patients with ICI-resistant melanoma. A separate phase 2 pivotal Agni-01 cohort is ongoing. ClinicalTrials.gov identifiers: NCT05470283 and NCT06060613 .

### [C] Non-invasive oral magnetoelectric vagus nerve neuromodulation for Parkinson's disease therapy.

- PMID: 42977739
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Nature biomedical engineering
- 作者: Mac CH, Chang PJ, Chu LA, Lin YH, Wang JT, Lin CY, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 03
- DOI: 10.1038/s41551-026-01829-x
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42977739/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-05

#### 摘要

Vagus nerve stimulation has shown promising neuroprotective and anti-inflammatory effects in progressive neurodegenerative disorders such as Parkinson's disease. However, its invasive nature introduces procedural and device-related risks. Here we introduce a magnetoelectric particulate system for oral delivery and non-invasive vagus nerve stimulation through the gut. The system comprises a magneto-responsive nickel ferrite core and a piezoelectric barium titanate shell, conjugated with capsaicin to specifically target TRPV1 receptors, ensuring effective adherence to the stomach lining after oral administration. Upon magnetic field stimulation, the particles generate electrical pulses that activate vagal afferent fibres, engaging the nucleus tractus solitarius in the brainstem via the gut-brain axis. This activation enhances norepinephrine production in the locus coeruleus and dopamine synthesis in the substantia nigra. Vagal efferent activation triggers the cholinergic anti-inflammatory pathway, mitigating both neuroinflammation and systemic inflammation. This non-invasive approach demonstrates potential for alleviating motor symptoms and non-motor anxiety in Parkinson's disease, offering valuable insights into its mechanisms and clinical applications.

### [C] Comparative Outcomes of Propranolol and Corticosteroids in Infantile Haemangiomas: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis.

- PMID: 42980098
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap

- 主题: 静脉畸形经典文献/综述补位
- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: British journal of hospital medicine (London, England : 2005)
- 作者: Sato A, Burns JWF, Sivanesan K, Haysom R, Riese D, Ghotra E, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct 28
- DOI: 10.31083/BJHM54357
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42980098/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-05

#### 摘要

AIMS/BACKGROUND: Infantile haemangiomas (IH) are the most common benign vascular tumours of infancy. Oral propranolol has become the first-line treatment, while corticosteroids remain second-line or adjunctive options when propranolol is contraindicated or insufficient. This meta-analysis provides updated comparative evidence on the efficacy and safety of oral propranolol versus oral and intralesional corticosteroids in paediatric IH.
METHODS: A systematic search of Ovid MEDLINE, Embase, Cochrane Library, CINAHL, and other databases was conducted on April 15, 2025, following Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-analyses (PRISMA) guidelines. Data were synthesised using a random-effects model.
RESULTS: Fifteen studies met inclusion criteria (n = 1841). Propranolol demonstrated a significantly higher overall treatment effectiveness compared to corticosteroids (odds ratio [OR]: 4.96, 95% confidence interval [CI]: 2.19-11.26; p = 0.0001). In subgroup analyses by route, propranolol was superior to oral corticosteroids (OR: 6.56, 95% CI: 1.98-21.73; p = 0.002), but showed no difference compared to intralesional corticosteroids. Overall adverse event odds were lower with propranolol (OR: 0.27, 95% CI: 0.10-0.71; p = 0.008), specifically when compared against oral corticosteroids (OR: 0.17, 95% CI: 0.03-0.88; p = 0.03) and in treatment-related ulceration rates (OR: 0.18, 95% CI: 0.08-0.42; p < 0.0001). No significant differences were observed between treatment groups regarding overall treatment duration, recurrence rate, or surgical resection rate.
CONCLUSION: Propranolol was associated with greater overall treatment effectiveness and a lower incidence of adverse events than corticosteroids. Greater effectiveness was observed compared with oral corticosteroids, but not intralesional corticosteroids. Intralesional corticosteroids may remain appropriate for selected patients, highlighting the importance of considering corticosteroid route and individual lesion characteristics when selecting treatment.
SYSTEMATIC REVIEW REGISTRATION: PROSPERO (CRD420251025754).

- 作者: Kadkhodayan Y, Yano M, Cross DT
- 发表日期: 2013 Oct 30
- DOI: 10.1136/bcr-2013-010844
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24172771/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-06

#### 摘要

A 58-year-old woman presented with a palpable mass at the base of the left neck which she had first noticed 12 years previously, shortly after abdominal surgery. The mass had progressively enlarged, resulting in dysphagia, dyspnea and occasional pain. Imaging showed a septated but otherwise simple cystic mass extending into the mediastinum and containing lymphocytic fluid on aspiration. A diagnosis of the rare entity of a thoracic duct cyst with supraclavicular extension was made. The patient opted for percutaneous sclerotherapy of the lesion, which was performed using glacial acetic acid. This resulted in complete resolution of the mass with one treatment. After exclusion of other causes of cystic left supraclavicular masses including cystic neoplasms and pseudoaneurysms of the carotid or subclavian arteries, direct puncture sclerotherapy can be safe and effective.

### [C] Harpagide alleviates neuroinflammation and cognitive dysfunction in sepsis-associated encephalopathy: Association with the MMP9/NF-κB signaling pathway.

- PMID: 42986892
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: PloS one
- 作者: Lou F, Deng G, Chen J, Jing Y, Wang A, Zhang M, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026
- DOI: 10.1371/journal.pone.0362296
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42986892/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-06

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Sepsis-associated encephalopathy (SAE) is a severe neurological complication of sepsis, characterized by uncontrolled neuroinflammation and cognitive decline, yet no specific therapy is currently available. Harpagide, a naturally occurring iridoid glycoside, has been reported to possess anti-inflammatory and neuroprotective activities; however, its therapeutic potential in SAE remains unexplored. This study aimed to investigate whether Harpagide exerts neuroprotective effects in SAE and to elucidate the underlying molecular mechanisms.
METHODS: A mouse model of SAE was established by intraperitoneal injection of lipopolysaccharide (LPS, 18 mg/kg). Cognitive performance was assessed using behavioral assays. Brain tissue morphology and neuronal survival were evaluated through histopathological examination. Glial cell activation and the production of pro-inflammatory cytokines (TNF-α, IL-1β, IL-6) were measured. Network pharmacology was employed to predict potential targets, followed by molecular docking and molecular dynamics simulations to predict binding interactions with core targets. Western blotting was performed to determine the expression levels of key proteins involved in the MMP9/NF-κB signaling pathway.
RESULTS: Treatment with Harpagide significantly improved behavioral performance, ameliorated brain pathological changes, preserved hippocampal neurons, suppressed glial activation, and reduced the levels of TNF-α, IL-1β, and IL-6 in SAE mice. Network pharmacology analysis predicted four core targets: GAPDH, CASP3, MMP9, and ICAM1. Molecular docking and molecular dynamics simulations suggested stable binding between Harpagide and MMP9. Western blot analysis further showed that Harpagide treatment was associated with reduced expression of MMP9 and phosphorylated NF-κB p65 in both hippocampal and cortical tissues.
CONCLUSIONS: The present study demonstrates that Harpagide exerts neuroprotective effects in SAE, which are associated with mitigated neuroinflammation and reduced MMP9/NF-κB signaling, supporting its potential as a promising therapeutic candidate for this devastating condition.

### [C] Tinospora cordifolia (Giloy) extract attenuates the D-Galactose-induced aging in mice via the AMPK-SIRT1 pathway.

- PMID: 42986551
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Molecular biology reports
- 作者: Singh B, Chaturvedi MM, Purohit JS
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 05
- DOI: 10.1007/s11033-026-13050-9
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42986551/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-06

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: D-Galactose is known to induce aging in animal models. Similarly, Tinospora cordifolia has been shown to have anti-aging properties in cell lines and model organisms. However, the biochemical pathway for the anti-aging effects of Tinospora cordifolia remains elusive. Further, no studies have demonstrated its anti-aging effects in the D-Galactose-induced aging model.
METHODS: The present study established an assay system for aging induction by injecting D-Galactose and subsequent attenuation of the induced aging by feeding ethanolic extracts of Tinospora cordifolia to mice via oral gavage. Following treatment, the mice were euthanised and liver, brain, spleen, kidney, testis and serum were harvested and used to assay oxidative biomarkers, inflammatory biomarkers and aging-related proteins.
RESULTS: The oxidative biomarkers, such as superoxide dismutase (SOD), glutathione (GSH) & catalase activities, were found to decline and the level of malondialdehyde (MDA) was enhanced in the naturally aged and induced aging mice groups, which were restored in the induced aging attenuated and Tinospora cordifolia-fed mice groups. Further, the increased levels of pro-inflammatory (Tumor necrosis factor-α-α and Interleukin-6) & multifunctional (Transforming growth fator-β1) cytokines and decreased levels of the anti-inflammatory (Interleukin-10) cytokines in the serum of the naturally aged and induced aging mice groups were restored in the induced aging attenuated and Tinospora cordifolia-fed mice groups. The western blotting results suggested an increased levels of the cell-cycle arrest proteins (p53, p21, and p16) and the pro-inflammatory transcription factor NFκB-p65 (Nuclear Factor kappa-light-chain-enhancer of activated B cells, protein 65) in the liver and testis of naturally aged and induced-aging mice groups, which was restored in the induced-aging-attenuated and Tinospora cordifolia-fed mice groups. Conversely, pro-longevity kinase AMPK (AMP-activated protein kinase), pro-longevity transcription factor FOXO1 (Forkhead box O1), & pro-longevity sirtuins were downregulated in the liver and testis of naturally aged and induced-aging mice groups, which were restored in the induced-aging-attenuated and Tinospora cordifolia-fed mice groups. However, similar trend in the change of protein levels were not observed in the brain tissues of these mice groups. CONCLUSIONS TINOSPORA CORDIFOLIA : attenuated the D-Galactose-induced aging via the AMPK-SIRT1 pathway in the liver and testis of the mice model, suggesting its potential anti-aging effects.

### [C] 7-Hydroxyflavone Attenuates Amyloid-β-Induced Alzheimer's Disease-Like Pathology Through Modulation of ROS/NLRP3 Inflammasome Signaling.

- PMID: 42986540
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap

- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Neurochemical research
- 作者: Rakshit D, Mishra A
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 05
- DOI: 10.1007/s11064-026-04942-0
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42986540/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-06

#### 摘要

Alzheimer's disease (AD), the widely recognised neurodegenerative disease with no proper cure to date, is typified by memory decline. Apart from the amyloid hypothesis and cholinergic dysfunction, neuroinflammation is also a key factor behind AD. 7-Hydroxyflavone (7HF), a flavonoid, possesses antioxidant, anti-inflammatory, and neuroprotective activities and remains unexplored in AD. Thus, in our study, we aimed to investigate the efficacy of 7HF against amyloid beta (Aβ)-induced AD in mice. Target acquisition for AD- and 7HF-related targets was performed, and gene ontology enrichment analysis was conducted using network pharmacology. 7HF was docked with AChE and NLRP3 proteins. The effect of 7HF was evaluated on Aβ-induced cytotoxicity, AChE activity and proinflammatory cytokines in Neuro2a (N2a) cells. Aβ1-42 was injected intracerebroventricularly in mice to induce AD, and mice were treated with 7HF (5, 10 & 20 mg/kg/day; p.o.) for 28 days. Behavioural assessments were performed during the last week of the study, followed by sacrifice and brain collection for further biochemical, molecular, and histological evaluations. 7HF shared targets with AD and exhibited a highly interconnected protein-protein network, with enriched ontology parameters linked to neuronal function. 7HF showed good binding affinity with AChE and NLRP3 proteins. It reduced Aβ-induced cytotoxicity, AChE activity, and proinflammatory cytokine levels in N2a cells. Treatment with 7HF improved the Aβ-induced memory deficits in mice. It also reduced pro-oxidant and AChE activity, as well as the molecular levels of NLRP3, ASC, cleaved caspase-1, and pro-inflammatory cytokines in the Aβ-induced mouse brain. Amyloid-associated structures in the Aβ-induced mouse brain were also reduced by 7HF intervention. These observations suggest that 7HF might have ameliorated AD-like pathology by reducing AChE activity, ROS levels, NLRP3-related neuroinflammatory markers, ultrastructural changes, and amyloid burden in Aβ-induced mice.

### [C] Neuroprotective Effects of Calcitriol after Experimental Ischemic Stroke induction via Modulation of Glutamate Excitotoxicity, MMP-9 Activity, and Oxidative Stress.

- PMID: 42982280
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article

- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Brain research bulletin
- 作者: Rahmatipour S, Bayat R, Rezvani Z, Vahidinia Z, Tameh AA
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 04
- DOI: 10.1016/j.brainresbull.2026.112204
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42982280/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-06

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Ischemic stroke triggers detrimental cascades, including glutamate excitotoxicity, oxidative stress, and neuroinflammation. This study evaluated the neuroprotective potential of calcitriol during the acute phase of stroke, focusing on inflammatory damage and modulation of glutamate transporters.
METHODS: Male Wistar rats underwent 1-h middle cerebral artery occlusion (MCAO) and were assigned to Sham, tMCAO + Vehicle, and tMCAO + Calcitriol groups. Calcitriol (1μg/kg) was administered at 30min, 24h, and 48h post-stroke. After 72h, behavioral outcomes, infarct volume, and histological alterations in cortical region were assessed utilizing Nissl staining. Cortical mRNA expression of glutamate-aspartate transporter (GLAST), glutamate transporter-1 (GLT-1), and excitatory amino acid transporter 3 (EAAT3) was evaluated via RT-PCR. Serum Matrix Metalloproteinase-9 (MMP-9) activity was measured using gelatin zymography, and oxidative stress biomarkers malondialdehyde (MDA), total antioxidant capacity (TAC), and nitric oxide (NO) were quantified in the penumbra. Molecular docking simulations explored calcitriol-MMP-9 binding affinity.
RESULTS: Calcitriol significantly reduced infarct volume, improved neurobehavioral scores, and mitigated morphological neuronal damage. It elevated TAC while suppressing MDA and NO levels. In line with docking simulations predicting a moderate binding affinity of -6.5kcal/mol, a marked decrease in serum MMP-9 enzymatic activity was observed. Furthermore, calcitriol treatment significantly reduced GLAST, GLT-1, and EAAT3 cortical gene expression compared to the untreated ischemia group.
CONCLUSION: Calcitriol administration attenuates acute ischemic brain injury. This neuroprotective effect is associated with parallel downregulations of glutamate transporter gene expression, suppression of penumbral oxidative stress, and reduced serum MMP-9 enzymatic activity.

### [C] ZeXieYin formula attenuates trimethylamine N-oxide- induced atherosclerosis and cognitive impairment by regulating microglial-neuronal signaling and autophagy.

- PMID: 42981748
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Journal of ethnopharmacology
- 作者: Wu W, He Y, Zhang X, Zhou S, Song Z, Hu Y, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 04
- DOI: 10.1016/j.jep.2026.122572
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42981748/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-06

#### 摘要

ETHNOPHARMACOLOGICAL RELEVANCE: ZeXieYin Formula (ZXYF), a classic traditional Chinese medicine decoction first documented in the Huangdi Neijing (an ancient Chinese medical text), traditionally used to treat patterns of internal accumulation of phlegm turbidity and deficiency of healthy qi-a pathogenesis closely associated with atherosclerosis and cognitive impairment, and which is composed of three medicinal herbs-Alisma plantago-aquatica L. (Zexie, Water Plantain Rhizome, dried tuber), Atractylodis macrocephala Koidz. (Baizhu, Largehead Atractylodes Rhizome, dried rhizome), and Pyrola calliantha Andres. (Luxiancao, Chinese Pyrola Herb, whole herb)-in a 2:2:1 ratio. For the preparation of ZXYF decoction, Zexie (200 g), Baizhu (200 g), and Luxiancao (100 g) were accurately weighed. ZXYF has been observed in our previous studies to reduce trimethylamine N-oxide (TMAO) levels, exert anti-inflammatory and lipid-lowering effects and alleviate atherosclerosis (AS) while improving cognitive function.
AIM OF THE STUDY: To investigate whether ZXYF ameliorates TMAO-induced AS with concurrent cognitive impairment (AS-CI) by modulating microglia-mediated paracrine signaling and subsequent neuroinflammation.
MATERIALS AND METHODS: To create an AS-CI model in vivo, Apolipoprotein E-deficient mice(ApoE-/- mice) were given a high-fat diet (containing the TMAO precursor choline) for 8 weeks. ZXYF (6.5, 13 g/kg) was administered via gavage. Aortic plaques were evaluated by Oil Red O staining, cognitive function was detected by behavioral tests (Y-maze, Morris water maze, and novel object recognition), and TMAO levels in brain tissue and serum were examined by Ultra-performance liquid chromatography-electrospray ionization tandem mass spectrometry (UPLC-ESI-MS/MS). Immunofluorescence, immunohistochemistry, and transmission electron microscopy (TEM) were used to evaluate microglial activation, C-C chemokine receptor type 5 (CCR5) expression, microtubule-associated protein 1 light chain 3 (LC3) expression, toxic proteins deposition, and autophagic ultrastructure in the hippocampus. Enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA) and Western blot were performed to detect the protein expression of chemokines, factors that promote inflammation, and related signaling pathways. In vitro, a paracrine model of BV2 microglia and HT22 neurons was established, and autophagic flux was detected by transfection with green fluorescent protein-red fluorescent protein-LC3 (GFP-RFP-LC3) lentivirus. CCR5 antagonist maraviroc, mTOR inhibitor rapamycin, and ZXYF-containing serum were used for intervention to verify the signaling pathway mechanism.
RESULTS: ZXYF treatment significantly reduced aortic plaque area, enhanced working, spatial, and recognition memory (p < 0.05), and concurrently lowered TMAO levels in both brain tissue and serum. ZXYF prevented hippocampus microglial activation and the MAPK/NF-κB inflammatory cascade, and downregulated the release of proinflammatory factors. Mechanistic studies showed that ZXYF markedly reduced the expression of chemokines C-C motif chemokine ligand 3 (CCL3), C-C motif chemokine ligand 4 (CCL4) and C-C motif chemokine ligand 5 (CCL5) in the brain, inhibited the expression of their receptor CCR5 and downstream mTOR phosphorylation, thereby restoring neuronal autophagy (increasing the number of autophagosomes, upregulating LC3-II and Beclin1, and downregulating sequestosome 1 (p62)) and reducing the deposition of amyloid-beta (Aβ) and phosphorylated Tau (p-Tau) proteins. In vitro experiments supported that the conditioned medium of activated microglia induced autophagy inhibition and neuroinflammation through the CCL3/4/5-CCR5-mTOR axis, while maraviroc, rapamycin and ZXYF-containing serum could block this effect and restore autophagic flux.
CONCLUSION: ZXYF ameliorates AS-CI by reducing TMAO levels, inhibiting microglial activation and the release of CCL3/4/5, thereby blocking the neuronal CCR5-mTOR signaling axis, restoring autophagic function and alleviating neuroinflammation.

### [C] Human iPSC-derived neural progenitor gene therapy improves outcomes in a neuropathic lysosomal disease.

- PMID: 42576387

- 作者: Mandolfo O, Learmonth YI, Usman A, Lorino M, Andreou T, Holley R, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 04
- DOI: 10.1016/j.ymthe.2026.08.004
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42576387/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-06

#### 摘要

Mucopolysaccharidosis type IIIA (MPS IIIA) is a neuropathic lysosomal storage disorder caused by SGSH mutations leading to heparan sulfate accumulation in the brain. This drives progressive cognitive decline, neuroinflammation, and behavioral abnormalities. No effective disease-modifying therapy exists, as current approaches fail to deliver sufficient enzyme to the brain or halt neurodegeneration. Here, we report a neural stem cell gene therapy that achieves high enzyme delivery and restores cognitive function, likely through integration of healthy neural cells across multiple brain regions. Induced pluripotent stem cells were generated from healthy and MPS IIIA patient fibroblasts, differentiated into neural progenitor cells, and transduced with lentiviral vectors encoding codon-optimized SGSH and luciferase. Modified cells were bilaterally transplanted into the striatum of NSG-MPSIIIA mice at 2 months of age. Three months post-injection, behavioral and histological analyses demonstrated full correction of spatial working memory deficits and significant reductions in heparan sulfate storage and astrogliosis. This allogeneic, enzyme-expressing neural cell strategy addresses both enzyme deficiency and neurodegeneration, offering a promising therapeutic avenue for MPS IIIA and broader lysosomal storage disorders.

### [C] Mitochondria-rich cytochalasomes as an intravenously deliverable vesicle platform for ischemic heart disease.

- PMID: 42936108
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence

- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: Bioactive materials
- 作者: Gao X, Zhang L, Xu L, Jin Y, Qian B, Zhang Y, et al.
- 发表日期: 2027 Mar
- DOI: 10.1016/j.bioactmat.2026.09.036
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42936108/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-06

#### 摘要

Poor organelle viability and rapid immune clearance limit mitochondrial transplantation for ischemic disease. Here, we engineer mitochondria-rich cytochalasomes (MRCs) as an intravenously deliverable vesicle platform. MRCs are generated through cytoskeletal remodeling in mesenchymal stem cells. They encapsulate functional mitochondria, protecting them from extracellular stressors and inheriting immunomodulatory proteins, which reduce macrophage phagocytosis by more than sixfold. Mechanistically, MRCs do not fuse with the host mitochondrial network but instead are trafficked to lysosomes and trigger protective autophagy-lysosomal response. In a murine myocardial ischemia/reperfusion (I/R) model, a single intravenous injection of MRCs reduced infarct size by 52% and preserved cardiac function for up to 28 days, whereas isolated mitochondria were ineffective. This work positions MRCs as a promising intravenously deliverable vesicle platform for ischemic heart disease and reveals a non-fusogenic mechanism.

### [C] Implanted and degradable hydrogel evaporative cooler for regional and repeated mild hypothermia therapy of traumatic brain injury.

- PMID: 42887234
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence

- 来源类型: 近期补位
- 期刊: Biomaterials
- 作者: Niu F, Gu J, Li M, Xu J, Chen Z, Qiu T, et al.
- 发表日期: 2027 Mar
- DOI: 10.1016/j.biomaterials.2026.124671
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42887234/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-06

#### 摘要

Implantable, regional and repeated mild hypothermia represents a promising therapeutic strategy for traumatic brain injury. Here, we develop a soft, degradable hydrogel microchannel evaporative cooler capable of removing heat through water evaporation in microchannels and achieving focused regional cooling within a short time. Fabricated from degradable hydrogels and using water as the coolant, the cooler shows biocompatibility and tissue adhesion. In vivo intracranial experiments with the cooler adhered on the rat dura mater enabled precise temperature regulation during repeated regional hypothermia (33 ± 0.5 °C, 4 h per day for 3 consecutive days). The treatment conferred neuroprotection and supported neural repair, with therapeutic effects persisting for 14 days. Sustained recovery was associated with increased Nestin+/Ki67+ and Sox2+/Ki67+ cells, along with elevated MAP2/PSD95 co-localization. Additional staining confirmed the presence of Nestin+/GFAP- and Sox2+/GFAP- cell populations, supporting the involvement of progenitor-like cells. At 28 days post-injury, rats receiving treatment exhibited reduced lesion volume, preserved axonal integrity and myelination, and ameliorated cognitive performance. Proteomic profiling performed at day 3 further suggested that repeated hypothermia modulates synaptic plasticity, dampens neuroinflammation, and rescues perturbed energy metabolism, representing a multi-faceted response distinct from single-episode hypothermia. Collectively, these findings highlight the translational potential of this implantable cooling strategy for TBI therapy.

### [C] Convergent neuroimmune signaling underlying rapid antidepressant response to ketamine and psychedelics.

- PMID: 42521762

- 作者: Jones GH, Gilbert JR, Johnston JN, Akula N, Schulmann A, Arakelian M, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Dec
- DOI: 10.1038/s41380-026-03777-z
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42521762/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-07

#### 摘要

Despite distinct receptor targets, both ketamine and serotonergic psychedelics produce a rapid clinical response and share biological signatures that suggest convergence on common downstream molecular mediators. To identify shared biomarkers of rapid antidepressant response, this study integrated CSF proteomics from healthy volunteers (HVs) who received intravenous ketamine with transcriptomic analyses from induced pluripotent stem cells (iPSCs) derived from participants with treatment-resistant depression (TRD) and HVs; iPSCs were treated with ketamine, its metabolite (2 R,6 R)-hydroxynorketamine, lysergic acid diethylamide (LSD), or psilocybin. Multimodal clinical characterization (transcriptomics (n = 16 TRD; 11 HV), magnetoencephalography (MEG) (n = 30 TRD; 25 HV), and plasma cytokines (n = 39 TRD; 25 HV) were also performed on TRD and HV participants who received a single dose of intravenous ketamine (0.5 mg/kg) or placebo. Conserved immune pathways were identified across CSF and iPSC neurons with interleukin-15 (IL)-15 and monocyte chemoattractant protein-1 (MCP-1) emerging as key regulatory hubs. Transcriptomically, in whole blood, ketamine responders exhibited decreased IL-15 and elevated B-cell signaling pathways at baseline that were reversed post-treatment. At the protein level, plasma IL-7 levels (primary B-cell driver) correlated with baseline MEG gamma power, reaching brain-wide significance across all participants (main effect pFDR < 0.05). The association was most pronounced in the TRD participants across subcortical regions (diagnosis x IL-7 pFDR < 10-14). Post-ketamine, the TRD IL-7-gamma relationship inverted, paralleling widespread gamma power reductions throughout default-mode network regions (session x IL-7 pclc < 0.05). In mixed-effects models, cytokine ratios linked to IL-7/IL-15 signaling predicted antidepressant response (IL-4/interferon gamma (IFN-γ) pFDR < 0.041) and non-response (MCP-1/IL-7 pFDR < 0.009), suggesting that rebalancing within the IL-7/IL-15 axis may contribute to therapeutic efficacy. Clinicaltrials.gov identifier: NCT00088699; NCT02484456.

### [C] The dual role of microglia in cognitive dysfunction: from neuroinflammation to therapeutic opportunities.

- PMID: 42988339
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Cross-domain vascular mechanism with exploratory CCM relevance
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 期刊: Neurobiology of disease
- 作者: Wang Y, Qiang C, Xu X, Zhou S, Liu C, Wang J
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 05
- DOI: 10.1016/j.nbd.2026.107678
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42988339/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-07

#### 摘要

Cognitive dysfunction is a major global burden. Microglia mediate neuroinflammation linking diverse etiologies to neuronal injury, with their functions extending beyond the brain parenchyma to include bidirectional crosstalk with the neurovascular unit. This review examines the dual role of microglia in cognitive disorders and therapeutic implications of their plasticity. Modern transcriptomic studies indicate that microglial states are multidimensional and context-dependent, rather than conforming to a simple M1/M2 dichotomy. Instead, microglia exist along a dynamic continuum encompassing homeostatic, disease-associated, and senescence-associated states. The relative contribution of protective and detrimental programs changes according to disease stage, anatomical location, pathological burden, and local cellular environment, and the two programs may coexist even within the same disease stage. Therapeutic strategies are therefore shifting from broad anti-inflammatory suppression toward precision modulation of microglial states. Their translational readiness varies from preclinical proof-of-concept to Phase II/III evaluation, and target engagement alone does not ensure restoration of physiological microglial function or cognitive benefit. Emerging interventions include TREM2 agonists, CSF1R modulators, PPARγ activators, NLRP3/STING/complement inhibitors, and microglia replacement. Key challenges include brain-targeted delivery and biomarker validation. Understanding microglia as plastic, context-dependent regulators may inform precision immunomodulatory therapies.

#### 摘要

Major depressive disorder (MDD) is a heterogeneous psychiatric disorder associated with oxidative stress, neuroinflammation, mitochondrial dysfunction, neuroendocrine dysregulation, and altered neuroplasticity. Bilirubin, an endogenous heme metabolite, exhibits antioxidant, anti-inflammatory, and cytoprotective properties in experimental systems, suggesting potential relevance to biological pathways implicated in depression. This review critically examines the biological rationale and clinical evidence linking bilirubin with depression and evaluates its potential translational significance. Clinical studies report heterogeneous associations between bilirubin concentrations and depressive outcomes across different populations, including MDD, postpartum depression, post-stroke depression, and individuals with diabetes. Some studies suggest inverse or potentially nonlinear associations, while findings vary according to population characteristics and clinical context. These observations may reflect differences in metabolic status, liver function, medication exposure, genetic variation, and other confounding factors. Proposed mechanisms involve oxidative and inflammatory signaling, mitochondrial function, neuroendocrine regulation, and vascular pathways; however, much of the mechanistic evidence derives from experimental models or populations other than individuals with MDD. Observational associations and experimental antioxidant effects do not establish that bilirubin causally protects against depression. Current evidence does not establish bilirubin as a validated diagnostic or prognostic biomarker for depression or as a causal protective factor. Bilirubin may warrant investigation as an adjunct within multimarker approaches, but its incremental clinical value remains uncertain. Larger longitudinal, multicenter, mechanistic, and multi-omics studies using standardized measurement methods are needed to clarify temporal and causal relationships, assess population-specific associations, and determine whether bilirubin contributes meaningfully to risk stratification or treatment monitoring.

### [C] Glioblastoma-associated microglia/macrophages (GAMs): Advances in knowledge and implications for glioblastoma (GBM) therapy.

- PMID: 42977100
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 期刊: Biochimica et biophysica acta. Reviews on cancer
- 作者: Singh S, Mishra R
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 03
- DOI: 10.1016/j.bbcan.2026.189742
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42977100/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-07

#### 摘要

Glioblastoma (GBM) is an exceptionally aggressive, therapy-resistant central nervous system malignancy. Within the tumour microenvironment, glioblastoma-associated microglia and macrophages (GAMs) constitute a highly heterogeneous population whose functional states profoundly influence disease progression, tissue architecture, immune evasion, and treatment response. In this review, we propose a conceptual framework in which distinct GAM phenotypes emerge from the integrated influence of vascular, tumoural, metabolic, biomechanical, neural, and immune inputs, together with glyco-immune interactions, extracellular vesicles, and epigenetic regulation. As GAMs process these cues, they actively remodel their surrounding ecosystem, sustaining a protumourigenic niche. We critically evaluate emerging therapeutic interventions-including those targeting GAM abundance, immunometabolism, mechanotransduction, and intercellular communication-as well as advanced platforms such as engineered myeloid systems, phytomedicines, targeted delivery vehicles, and gut-brain axis-based strategies. Furthermore, we address key translational bottlenecks, including extreme GAM plasticity, spatial heterogeneity, limitations of preclinical models, barriers to central nervous system delivery, and a lack of predictive biomarkers. We argue that the therapeutically relevant target is not the GAM population in isolation, but rather the diverse niche-GAM functional units and the cumulative signalling networks that sustain them. Ultimately, achieving durable clinical efficacy will require biomarker-guided, spatially informed combination regimens that selectively disrupt maladaptive myeloid states while preserving physiological microglial homeostasis.

### [C] Anthracycline Cardiotoxicity as Multicellular Network Failure: Cardiac Niche Reprogramming and Pathological Cell-Cell Communication.

- PMID: 42924836
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Cross-domain vascular mechanism with exploratory CCM relevance
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 期刊: Journal of the American Heart Association
- 作者: Wang S, Ding Q, Ding S, Liu C, Li Q
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 03
- DOI: 10.1161/JAHA.126.053190
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42924836/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-07

#### 摘要

Anthracyclines are essential anticancer agents, yet a cardiomyocyte-centered model incompletely explains why cardiac injury can be delayed, progressive, and poorly captured by resting ejection fraction. We frame anthracycline cardiotoxicity as a multicellular network failure within the cardiac niche: the spatially organized interactions among cardiomyocytes, fibroblasts, endothelial and immune cells, extracellular matrix, and circulating signals. Cardiomyocyte stress initiates injury, whereas pathological communication may propagate it. We distinguish a fibroblast-CD8+ T-cell adhesion circuit, supported by loss-of-function evidence in mice and correlative human data, from an emerging therapy-conditioned tumor-heart extracellular vesicle axis supported mainly by breast cancer models. These pathways may converge on fibrotic stiffness, microvascular dysfunction, and electrical uncoupling; however, direct cross-circuit interaction and drug-independent sufficiency remain unproven. We integrate established clinical risk modifiers with this framework, specify causal criteria, and propose spatial multiomics, living myocardial slices, tumor-bearing systems, and longitudinal phenotyping to test circuit persistence. This evidence-calibrated model shifts the goal from cataloging cell-intrinsic pathways to identifying mechanism-resolved biomarkers and therapeutically targetable communication nodes while preserving antitumor efficacy.

### [C] Nanoparticle-Mediated Therapies for Atherosclerosis: A Novel Approach to Targeted Treatment.

- PMID: 42969214
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory
- 转化相关性依据: Cross-domain vascular mechanism with exploratory CCM relevance

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 经典补位
- 期刊: Drug development and industrial pharmacy
- 作者: Rangnekar T, Londhe V
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 02
- DOI: 10.1080/03639045.2026.2758725
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42969214/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-07

#### 摘要

INTRODUCTION: Atherosclerosis(AS) remains a leading cause of global cardiovascular mortality due to the formation of lipid rich plaques and chronic vascular inflammation. Conventional therapies, including statins, antiplatelet agents and surgical interventions, primarily control risk factors by preventing new plaque deposits but show limited ability to selectively target inflamed vascular sites.
OBJECTIVE: This review critically evaluates current advances in nanoparticle mediated drug delivery for atherosclerosis, with emphasis on mechanistic targeting of inflamed plaques, modulation of macrophage driven inflammatory and oxidative pathways within the diseased vascular microenvironment. This review also highlights comparative analysis of major nanoparticle systems, integration of theranostic functionalities for simultaneous molecular imaging and therapy. The translational barriers are also addressed to enable clinical implementation.Significance of Review: Despite advancements in lipid-lowering and anti-inflammatory drugs, current atherosclerosis treatments are limited by inadequate plaque specificity, rapid systemic clearance, off-target toxicity, and persistent cardiovascular risk. Nanoparticle based systems can solve these problems with simultaneous diagnosis and therapy, thereby addressing major unmet clinical needs.Key Findings: Different types of nanoparticles demonstrate promising therapeutic outcomes by modulating macrophage activity, reducing oxidative stress, enabling controlled drug release and improving plaque imaging. Theranostic nanoparticles further allow simultaneous diagnosis and treatment. However, large-scale manufacturing, nanotoxicology and regulatory constraints continue to hinder clinical translation.
CONCLUSIONS: Nanoparticle-mediated therapies represent a transformative approach for atherosclerosis by enabling precision, targeted and multifunctional treatment. Continued optimization of safety, scalability and regulatory compliance will be essential for successful clinical adoption and improved patient outcomes.

### [C] Surgical Management of Pediatric Foot Macrodactyly: A Case Series Highlighting Variable Growth Patterns and Soft-Tissue Challenges.

- PMID: 42991178
- Domain: Out-of-scope
