- 作者: Shintani K, Nakagawa K, Hosomi R, Onishi Y, Ohira C, Terai H
- 发表日期: 2026 Oct
- DOI: 10.7759/cureus.117508
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42991178/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-08

#### 摘要

Macrodactyly of the foot is a rare congenital anomaly characterized by disproportionate overgrowth of osseous and soft-tissue structures. Its clinical course varies considerably among patients, and no standardized surgical strategy has been established. We retrospectively reviewed four pediatric patients with foot macrodactyly who underwent surgical treatment at our institutions. The series included two patients without clinical features suggestive of an associated overgrowth syndrome and two with associated overgrowth syndromes. Surgical procedures were selected according to the extent and progression of the deformity and included epiphysiodesis, phalangectomy, distal toe amputation, ray resection, metatarsal shortening osteotomy, and soft-tissue debulking. One patient showed relatively limited subsequent enlargement after early surgical treatment and required no further surgery, whereas the other patients required additional procedures for progressive or residual deformity. In the two patients with associated overgrowth syndromes, marked soft-tissue hypertrophy remained a major therapeutic challenge despite surgical treatment, including soft-tissue debulking. These cases demonstrate that surgical management of foot macrodactyly should consider not only growth behavior but also the three-dimensional characteristics of the deformity. Longitudinal and transverse enlargement can be addressed by growth modulation and skeletal procedures, whereas vertical enlargement caused predominantly by soft-tissue hypertrophy may be more difficult to control. A staged and individualized surgical strategy with long-term follow-up is therefore important, particularly in patients with progressive deformity or marked soft-tissue involvement.

### [C] IGFBP7-targeting monoclonal antibodies attenuate the endothelial inflammatory niche in psoriasis-like inflammation.

- PMID: 42992544
- Domain: Cross-domain vascular biology
- Domain 依据: Transferable mechanism appears with explicit vascular/endothelial context
- 转化相关性: Exploratory

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Autoimmunity
- 作者: Zhang X, Zhang T, Xu J, Li K, Li G, Miao H, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Dec 31
- DOI: 10.1080/08916934.2026.2756737
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42992544/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-08

#### 摘要

Psoriasis is sustained by immune-endothelial interactions that support leukocyte recruitment into inflamed skin. We previously showed that endothelial IGFBP7 disrupts the glycocalyx and promotes T-cell extravasation. Here, we developed and compared monoclonal antibodies against human IGFBP7. Reanalysis of longitudinal single-cell RNA sequencing from risankizumab-treated psoriasis skin identified an IGFBP7-positive vascular endothelial state that was enriched in baseline lesions and reduced during treatment. Cutaneous IGFBP7 expression also correlated with Psoriasis Area and Severity Index in an independent cohort. Three mouse monoclonal antibodies were generated against recombinant human IGFBP7, and all three recognized recombinant murine IGFBP7. CMC0667 showed the strongest reference-antibody cross-competition (IC₅₀, 5.72 nM), while surface plasmon resonance confirmed direct nanomolar-range binding to IGFBP7 (k a , 2.86 × 105 M-1 s-1; k d , 1.35 × 10-2 s-1; kinetic K D , 47.4 nM). In cytokine-stimulated HMEC-1 cells, the antibodies increased hyaluronic acid and heparan sulfate signals, reduced endothelial activation, improved junction-associated gene expression, and decreased FITC-dextran permeability and CD3⁺CD4⁺ T-cell transendothelial migration. In imiquimod-treated mice, all three antibodies reduced epidermal hyperplasia, keratinocyte proliferation, inflammatory and chemotactic gene expression, and cutaneous T-cell accumulation. CMC0667 showed the most consistent overall activity. Together, these data identify CMC0667 as the lead anti-IGFBP7 antibody for further preclinical optimization.

### [C] Identification of potential active ingredients from Chuanxiong Rhizoma in the treatment of vascular dementia: CHI3L1 and TLR2 are potential key targets.

- PMID: 42994846
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low

- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Medicine
- 作者: Liao H, Zeng Q, Zhang J, Chen T, Li J, He Q, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 06
- DOI: 10.1097/MD.0000000000051414
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42994846/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-08

#### 摘要

Vascular dementia (VaD) is a cognitive impairment resulting from cerebrovascular disease, which causes functional decline. Its rising prevalence and the absence of effective therapeutic interventions highlight an urgent need for novel therapeutic strategies. The research aimed to identify crucial target genes and bioactive compounds responsible for the therapeutic effects of Chuanxiong Rhizoma in VaD through a comprehensive bioinformatics strategy. Network pharmacology identified 240 shared target genes between Chuanxiong Rhizoma and VaD. Weighted gene co-expression network analysis identified 114 genes significantly associated with VaD. Venn analysis identified CHI3L1, TAC1, toll-like receptor 2 (TLR2), and CCL2 as candidate target genes. Differential expression analysis confirmed dysregulation of CHI3L1, TAC1, TLR2, and CCL2 in VaD. Single-cell RNA analysis showed CHI3L1 predominantly in fibrous astrocytes, TAC1 in Parvalbumin inhibitory neurons, and TLR2 in microglia. Receiver operating characteristic analysis showed CHI3L1 (area under the curve [AUC] = 0.6597, P = .0144), TAC1 (AUC = 0.7797, P < .0001), and TLR2 (AUC = 0.7468, P = .0002) had significant AUC values, indicating potential diagnostic value for VaD. Gene set enrichment analysis showed CHI3L1, TAC1, and TLR2 enriched in chemokine signaling, allograft rejection, and JAK/STAT pathways. Immune cell infiltration analysis indicated that the correlation of CHI3L1 with Macrophages M2 was significantly positive, whereas it was significantly negative with activated natural killer cells (NK cells). The correlation of TAC1 with Macrophages M2 was significantly negative, whereas it was significantly positive with both activated NK cells and follicular helper T cells. The correlation of TLR2 with Macrophages M2 was significantly positive, whereas it was significantly negative with activated NK cells and follicular helper T cells. Molecular docking followed by 100-nanosecond molecular dynamics simulations indicated that palmitic acid binds stably to both CHI3L1 and TLR2, and that perillyl alcohol binds stably to TLR2, whereas the CHI3L1-perillyl alcohol complex showed persistent instability during the molecular dynamics trajectory and was therefore not considered a supported interaction. The findings indicated Chuanxiong Rhizoma might exert its anti-VaD effects through palmitic acid and perillyl alcohol by targeting CHI3L1 and TLR2. The study generated a multi-component, multi-target hypothesis for VaD that warrants further experimental validation.

### [C] Ghrelin treatment is associated with attenuated microglial inflammation and preserved mitochondrial integrity after cerebral ischemia/reperfusion injury.

- PMID: 42994632
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low
- 转化相关性依据: Only generic or out-of-scope keyword overlap
- 优先级依据: C: broad, exploratory, low-relevance, or unmatched article
- 主题: 神经免疫/炎症治疗
- 来源类型: 今日新文献
- 期刊: Neurotoxicology and teratology
- 作者: Chuan L, Wen S, Wang J, Ren J, Peng H, Gao Q, et al.
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 06
- DOI: 10.1016/j.ntt.2026.107727
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42994632/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-08

#### 摘要

BACKGROUND: Cerebral ischemia/reperfusion injury (CI/RI) causes neurological disability. Microglial inflammation and mitochondrial apoptosis are key pathological events. Ghrelin's role in these processes following CI/RI remains unclear.
OBJECTIVES: To examine whether ghrelin treatment is associated with reduced microglial inflammation and preserved mitochondrial integrity in CI/RI models, using in vitro OGD/R primary microglia and in vivo MCAO rat models.
METHODS: Primary microglia underwent OGD/R (8 h/24 h) with ghrelin (0.1-1 mM). Cell viability, apoptosis, GHSR, inflammatory cytokines (TNF-α, IL-6, IL-8), ROS, ATP, mitochondrial membrane potential (ΔΨm), and ultrastructure were measured. Rats underwent MCAO (2 h/24 h) and received ghrelin (50 μg/kg/d, i.p.) or saline for 7 days. Infarct volume, histology, IBA-1, mitochondrial complexes I/IV, cytochrome c distribution, NF-κB p65 translocation, TNF-α/IL-1β/IL-6, and Bcl-2/Bax/Caspase-9 were assessed.
RESULTS: In OGD/R-exposed microglia, ghrelin dose-dependently attenuated viability loss and apoptosis, restored GHSR, reduced TNF-α/IL-6/IL-8, lowered ROS, increased ATP, and preserved ΔΨm and ultrastructure. In MCAO rats, ghrelin treatment was associated with smaller infarct volume, less neuronal damage, lower IBA-1, restored ATP and complexes I/IV, increased mitochondrial cytochrome c retention, reduced NF-κB p65 nuclear translocation, decreased TNF-α/IL-1β/IL-6, and altered Bcl-2/Bax/Caspase-9 (higher Bcl-2 and full-length Caspase-9, lower Bax).
CONCLUSION: In both models, ghrelin treatment correlates with attenuated microglial inflammation and preserved mitochondrial integrity. These findings indicate a potential protective association of ghrelin against CI/RI.

### [C] Gasdermin, inflammatory, and heat-shock gene expression profiles in the naturally aged male mouse brain following propolis and dexpanthenol treatment.

- PMID: 42993343
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence

- 作者: Şahin U
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 06
- DOI: 10.1007/s10522-026-10539-5
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42993343/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-08

#### 摘要

Aging is accompanied by alterations in inflammatory signaling, regulated cell-death pathways, and cellular stress responses in the brain. This study investigated the effects of propolis and dexpanthenol, administered individually or in combination, on gasdermin-related, inflammatory, and heat-shock gene expression in naturally aged male mice. Thirty-five male CD-1 mice were randomly allocated to five groups (n = 7/group): Young Control, Aged Control, Aged + Propolis, Aged + Dexpanthenol, and Aged + Propolis + Dexpanthenol. Propolis (200 mg/kg/day) and dexpanthenol (500 mg/kg/day) were administered for 14 days. Whole-brain mRNA expression of GSDMD, GSDME, GSDMC, TNF-α, IL-1β, HSP70, and HSP90 was evaluated by quantitative real-time PCR. Natural aging was associated with marked reductions in TNF-α, IL-1β, and HSP70 expression, whereas GSDMD, GSDME, GSDMC, and HSP90 were not significantly altered after correction for multiple comparisons. Propolis reduced GSDME and IL-1β expression while increasing GSDMC relative to aged controls. Dexpanthenol increased GSDMD and HSP70 expression, shifted TNF-α toward the young-control profile, and reduced HSP90 expression. Combined administration produced a distinct transcriptional profile characterized by pronounced increases in GSDMD, TNF-α, IL-1β, and HSP70 relative to aged controls. These findings indicate that propolis and dexpanthenol are associated with distinct, gene-specific transcriptional responses in the naturally aged brain rather than uniform changes across the inflammatory and gasdermin-related genes examined. Because the analyses were restricted to mRNA expression, the observed gasdermin transcriptional changes do not establish functional pyroptosis. The prominent HSP70 response further suggests that cellular stress adaptation and proteostasis may represent important components of these treatment-associated molecular changes.

- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42993338/

#### 摘要

To investigate molecular changes associated with the protective effect of Tongqiao Huashuan Decoction (THD) in vascular dementia (VD), focusing on the NF‑κB‑related inflammatory response, and to observe its influence on intestinal flora. The components of THD were identified using Liquid chromatography‑quadrupole time‑of‑flight tandem mass spectrometry (LC‑Q‑TOF‑MS/MS) with electrospray ionization in positive/negative modes, a methanol‑0.1% formic acid gradient mobile phase, and a C18 column (1.7 μm, 2.1 × 100 mm). Sprague‑Dawley rats were divided into four groups: Sham, VD, VD + TCM (THD), and VD+Donepezil (donepezil hydrochloride). Spatial learning and memory were assessed using the Morris Water Maze. Transmission electron microscopy (TEM) analyzed the ultrastructure of hippocampal CA1 neurons. Enzyme‑linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA) detected interleukin‑1β (IL‑1β), tumor necrosis factor‑α (TNF‑α), macrophage inflammatory protein‑2 (MIP‑2), and IL‑6 in the hippocampus and serum. Immunohistochemistry (IHC) and Western blotting (WB) measured the expression of sirtuin 1 (SIRT1), NF‑κB, caspase‑1, and NOD‑like receptor protein 3 (NLRP3). Fecal flora was analyzed via 16S rRNA sequencing. LC‑Q‑TOF‑MS/MS detected 998 chemical constituents in THD extract. Among them, 24 high‑signal‑intensity constituents with mass‑spectrometry response values > 100 000 were tentatively annotated based on exact‑mass and MS/MS fragmentation matching against public databases and published literature. Compared with the Sham group, VD rats exhibited longer escape latency, hippocampal neuronal damage, elevated proinflammatory factors, upregulated caspase‑1/NLRP3/NF‑κB, and downregulated SIRT1/IκBα. THD treatment reversed these VD‑induced pathological abnormalities. In addition, THD restored intestinal flora dysbiosis observed in VD model rats. THD ameliorates VD‑related cognitive dysfunction, accompanied by altered expression of SIRT1/IκBα/NF‑κB‑related proteins and reduced pro‑inflammatory cytokines. Meanwhile, THD can restore disturbed gut microbiota in VD rats. Changes in the SIRT1/IκBα/NF‑κB signaling cascade are therefore associated with THD‑mediated protective outcomes.

### [C] Proanthocyanidin ameliorates BCCAO-induced neuroinflammation in cerebral ischemia-reperfusion injury (CIRI) in rats by targeting NF-κB/NLRP3 inflammasome pathway.

- PMID: 42993218
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence

- 期刊: Naunyn-Schmiedeberg's archives of pharmacology
- 作者: Pachauri PK, Panda SP
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 06
- DOI: 10.1007/s00210-026-06012-2
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42993218/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-08

#### 摘要

Cerebral ischemia-reperfusion injury (CIRI) is a major cause of mortality and long-term neurological disability, characterized by excessive oxidative stress, neuroinflammation, and neuronal degeneration following the restoration of cerebral blood flow. The present study investigated the neuroprotective effects of proanthocyanidin (PAC) against bilateral common carotid artery occlusion (BCCAO)-induced cerebral ischemia in Wistar albino rats, with particular emphasis on the NF-κB/NLRP3 inflammasome signaling pathway. Rats subjected to BCCAO received PAC at two dose levels or standard therapy (aspirin + clopidogrel). Neurological and cognitive functions were evaluated using the rotarod, actophotometer, and elevated plus maze tests. Biochemical assays were performed to determine oxidative stress markers and antioxidant enzyme activities, while histopathological examination assessed neuronal integrity. The molecular mechanism was further investigated by Western blot analysis and densitometric quantification of NF-κB (p65), NLRP3, and caspase-1 protein expression. BCCAO significantly impaired motor coordination, locomotor activity, and memory retention, accompanied by increased lipid peroxidation, myeloperoxidase, and acetylcholinesterase activities, depletion of endogenous antioxidants (glutathione, superoxide dismutase, and catalase), and marked neuronal degeneration in the hippocampal and cortical regions. PAC treatment significantly improved behavioral performance, restored antioxidant defense, reduced oxidative stress and biochemical alterations, and preserved neuronal architecture in a dose-dependent manner. Western blot analysis demonstrated that PAC markedly downregulated the overexpression of NF-κB (p65), NLRP3, and caspase-1 proteins, indicating suppression of NF-κB/NLRP3 inflammasome-mediated neuroinflammation. Overall, PAC exhibited significant neuroprotective efficacy comparable to aspirin plus clopidogrel, suggesting that its beneficial effects are mediated through the attenuation of oxidative stress and the inhibition of NF-κB/NLRP3 inflammasome signaling. These findings support PAC as a promising phytotherapeutic candidate for the management of cerebral ischemia-reperfusion injury.

### [C] Mapping HIV reservoir geography: lessons for cure from the Last Gift rapid autopsy cohort.

- PMID: 42989938
- Domain: Out-of-scope
- Domain 依据: No strong CCM/vascular-malformation or transferable vascular mechanism evidence
- 转化相关性: Low

- 作者: Birabaharan M, Immer A, Gomez AN, Valdes D, Smith DM
- 发表日期: 2026 Nov 06
- DOI: 10.1097/COH.0000000000001079
- PubMed: https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42989938/
- 日报日期: 2026-11-08

#### 摘要

PURPOSE OF REVIEW: Deep tissue reservoirs remain a major barrier to HIV cure. Their anatomical distribution and connectivity are difficult to study in living people. We examine how the Last Gift rapid-autopsy cohort clarifies reservoir connectivity, persistence behind anatomical barriers, and variation within organs, and consider the implications for cure.
RECENT FINDINGS: Tissue studies reveal HIV reservoirs that are interconnected across yet differ between and within anatomical sites. Viral sequence analyses suggest that blood links tissue reservoirs, with gut and lymphoid tissues acting as hubs of viral dispersal. Recovery of replication-competent HIV from brain microglia during suppressive antiretroviral therapy (ART) provides direct evidence of a resident brain reservoir. Even within the gastrointestinal tract, HIV DNA burden and transcriptions vary by region, with different microbiome associations in the small and large bowel.
SUMMARY: HIV reservoir geography supports durable ART-free remission as a practical goal. Achieving this goal will likely require combination therapies with complementary functions and tissue access. Because tissue heterogeneity limits what negative biopsies can establish, remission should be assessed through carefully monitored analytical treatment interruption and long-term follow up.

#### 摘要

Gut microbiota modulates gut-brain crosstalk in Parkinson's disease (PD), yet effective therapies are still lacking. Herein, we report an orally administrable microalgae-nanozyme platform (SP@SKCN) via combining sulfur/potassium (S/K) co-doped carbon nitride (SKCN) with Spirulina platensis (SP), offering a novel therapeutic strategy for PD. Uniform S-K co-doping significantly narrows the bandgap of carbon nitride (2.2 eV), endowing SKCN with high near-infrared (NIR)-photocatalytic activity and a star-shaped network morphology, realizing NIR-driven water splitting for H2/O2 generation. Meanwhile, SKCN exhibits superoxide dismutase (SOD)/catalase (CAT) mimetic enzyme activities, efficiently scavenging reactive oxygen species (ROS) and synergistically alleviating inflammation. SP incorporation protects SP@SKCN from gastric acid degradation, prolongs its intestinal retention time, and mediates sustained SKCN release. Notably, SP@SKCN remarkedly improves motor deficits, gastrointestinal dysfunctions, and dopaminergic neuron loss in PD mice. 16 S rRNA sequencing and targeted metabolomics reveal that SP@SKCN treatment reverses both PD-associated microbiota changes and the MPTP-induced reduction in indole-3-lactic acid (ILA). Further experiments indicate that SP@SKCN alleviate intestinal inflammation and gut barrier disruption, consequently attenuating systemic inflammation. Mechanistically, SP@SKCN restore the ILA/aryl hydrocarbon receptor (AHR)/NF-κB signaling caused by disturbed gut microbiota mediated the glial cells activation, which eventually reduce dopaminergic neuron death. Collectively, SP@SKCN ameliorates PD via regulating the microbiota-ILA-brain axis.
